[Enabled Toggle Names]
----------------------
*   lazy_clear_resource_on_first_use: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/145):
    Clears resource to zero on first usage. This initializes the resource so
    that no dirty bits from recycled memory is present in the new resource.

*   use_d3d12_resource_heap_tier2: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/27):
    Enable support for resource heap tier 2. Resource heap tier 2 allows
    mixing of texture and buffers in the same heap. This allows better heap
    re-use and reduces fragmentation.

*   use_d3d12_render_pass: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/36):
    Use the D3D12 render pass API introduced in Windows build 1809 by default.
    On versions of Windows prior to build 1809, or when this toggle is turned
    off, Dawn will emulate a render pass.

*   use_d3d12_residency_management: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/193):
    Enable residency management. This allows page-in and page-out of resource
    heaps in GPU memory. This component improves overcommitted performance by
    keeping the most recently used resources local to the GPU. Turning this
    component off can cause allocation failures when application memory
    exceeds physical device memory.

*   disallow_spirv: 
    (https://crbug.com/1214923):
    Disallow usage of SPIR-V completely so that only WGSL is used for shader
    modules. This is useful to prevent a Chromium renderer process from
    successfully sending SPIR-V code to be compiled in the GPU process.

*   timestamp_quantization: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/1800):
    Enable timestamp queries quantization to reduce the precision of timers
    that can be created with timestamp queries.

*   apply_clear_big_integer_color_value_with_draw: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/537):
    Apply the clear value of the color attachment with a draw call when load
    op is 'clear'. This toggle is enabled by default on D3D12 backends when we
    set large integer values (> 2^24 or < -2^24 for signed integer formats) as
    the clear value of a color attachment with 32-bit integer or unsigned
    integer formats because D3D12 APIs only support using float numbers as
    clear values, while a float number cannot always precisely represent an
    integer that is greater than 2^24 or smaller than -2^24). This toggle is
    also enabled on Intel GPUs on Metal backend due to a driver issue on Intel
    Metal driver.

*   disable_sub_allocation_for_2d_texture_with_copy_dst_or_render_attachment: 
    (https://crbug.com/1237175):
    Disable resource sub-allocation for the 2D texture with CopyDst or
    RenderAttachment usage. Due to driver issues, this toggle is enabled by
    default on D3D12 backends using Intel Gen9.5 or Gen11 GPUs, on Vulkan
    backends using Intel Gen12 GPUs, and D3D12 backends using AMD GPUs.

*   d3d12_use_root_signature_version_1_1: 
    (https://crbug.com/tint/1890):
    Use D3D12 Root Signature Version 1.1 to make additional guarantees about
    the descriptors in a descriptor heap and the data pointed to by the
    descriptors so that the drivers can make better optimizations on them.

*   d3d12_use_64kb_alignment_msaa_texture: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/282):
    Create MSAA textures with 64KB
    (D3D12_SMALL_MSAA_RESOURCE_PLACEMENT_ALIGNMENT) alignment.

*   d3d12_create_not_zeroed_heap: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/484):
    Create D3D12 heap with D3D12_HEAP_FLAG_CREATE_NOT_ZEROED when it is
    supported. It is safe because in Dawn we always clear the resources
    manually when needed.

*   polyfill_packed_4x8_dot_product: 
    (https://crbug.com/tint/1497):
    Always use the polyfill version of dot4I8Packed() and dot4U8Packed().

*   d3d12_polyfill_pack_unpack_4x8: 
    (https://crbug.com/tint/1497):
    Always use the polyfill version of pack4xI8(), pack4xU8(),
    pack4xI8Clamp(), unpack4xI8() and unpack4xU8() on D3D12 backends. Note
    that these functions are always polyfilled on all other backends right
    now.

*   d3d12_relax_buffer_texture_copy_pitch_and_offset_alignment: 
    (https://crbug.com/381000081):
    Don't require the alignments of D3D12_TEXTURE_DATA_PITCH_ALIGNMENT (256)
    for row pitch and D3D12_TEXTURE_DATA_PLACEMENT_ALIGNMENT (512) for offset
    in buffer-texture copies.

*   enable_integer_range_analysis_in_robustness: 
    (https://crbug.com/348701956):
    Compute the range of the index with Integer Range Analysis in the
    robustness transform and skip doing index clamping when the out of bound
    access cannot happen.

*   blob_cache_hash_validation: 
    (https://crbug.com/429938352):
    Enable hash validation when loading/storing from/to the blob cache

*   collapse_subgroup_min_max: 
    (https://crbug.com/508265321):
    Collapse redundant subgroup min and max operations (e.g.,
    subgroupMin(subgroupMin(x))) into a single operation. This works around a
    driver crash on some AMD GPUs.

*   d3d12_use_hlsl_2021: 
    (https://crbug.com/508342536):
    Use HLSL 2021 when targeting DXC.

    (https://crbug.com/1214923):
    Disallow usage of SPIR-V completely so that only WGSL is used for shader
    modules. This is useful to prevent a Chromium renderer process from
    successfully sending SPIR-V code to be compiled in the GPU process.

*   timestamp_quantization:

[Enabled Toggle Names]
----------------------
*   lazy_clear_resource_on_first_use: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/145):
    Clears resource to zero on first usage. This initializes the resource so
    that no dirty bits from recycled memory is present in the new resource.

*   use_d3d12_resource_heap_tier2: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/27):
    Enable support for resource heap tier 2. Resource heap tier 2 allows
    mixing of texture and buffers in the same heap. This allows better heap
    re-use and reduces fragmentation.

*   use_d3d12_render_pass: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/36):
    Use the D3D12 render pass API introduced in Windows build 1809 by default.
    On versions of Windows prior to build 1809, or when this toggle is turned
    off, Dawn will emulate a render pass.

*   use_d3d12_residency_management: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/193):
    Enable residency management. This allows page-in and page-out of resource
    heaps in GPU memory. This component improves overcommitted performance by
    keeping the most recently used resources local to the GPU. Turning this
    component off can cause allocation failures when application memory
    exceeds physical device memory.

*   disallow_spirv: 
    (https://crbug.com/1214923):
    Disallow usage of SPIR-V completely so that only WGSL is used for shader
    modules. This is useful to prevent a Chromium renderer process from
    successfully sending SPIR-V code to be compiled in the GPU process.

*   timestamp_quantization: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/1800):
    Enable timestamp queries quantization to reduce the precision of timers
    that can be created with timestamp queries.

*   apply_clear_big_integer_color_value_with_draw: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/537):
    Apply the clear value of the color attachment with a draw call when load
    op is 'clear'. This toggle is enabled by default on D3D12 backends when we
    set large integer values (> 2^24 or < -2^24 for signed integer formats) as
    the clear value of a color attachment with 32-bit integer or unsigned
    integer formats because D3D12 APIs only support using float numbers as
    clear values, while a float number cannot always precisely represent an
    integer that is greater than 2^24 or smaller than -2^24). This toggle is
    also enabled on Intel GPUs on Metal backend due to a driver issue on Intel
    Metal driver.

*   d3d12_use_64kb_alignment_msaa_texture: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/282):
    Create MSAA textures with 64KB
    (D3D12_SMALL_MSAA_RESOURCE_PLACEMENT_ALIGNMENT) alignment.

*   d3d12_create_not_zeroed_heap: 
    (https://crbug.com/dawn/484):
    Create D3D12 heap with D3D12_HEAP_FLAG_CREATE_NOT_ZEROED when it is
    supported. It is safe because in Dawn we always clear the resources
    manually when needed.

*   polyfill_packed_4x8_dot_product:

# APPENDIX D — NEGATIVE-REFERENCE EVIDENCE NOTES (13 AUGUST 2026)

## D1. Vantum Studio

Current indexed live content independently confirms the structural patterns discussed in Part II: a conventional landing-page sequence with hero metrics, “why”/problem/process labels, numbered steps, service lists, included extras, pricing cards, FAQ, about/values, repeated CTAs, contact form and utility footer. It also confirms the heavy use of short contrastive marketing phrases, repeated reassurance, numerical claims and the section-label hierarchy that functions like eyebrow copy.

The user’s direct visual observation additionally identifies the gray/AI-template visual language and cheap microanimation style as negative evidence. Exact CSS/easing/framework values were not directly replayed in this compilation and are therefore not fabricated.

## D2. Vantum Works

The main studio identifies itself as a software studio spanning multiple product categories. The current text environment does not reliably expose all product pages or their complete rendered visual behavior.

**Verified public product:** Link Shrink, which exposes QR/link capabilities, analytics sample data, use cases, pricing tiers, plan comparison and a conventional product/resources footer.

**Partially discoverable:** Scout metadata describes structured recruiter screening/intake; Piper appears in indexing evidence but was not sufficiently rendered for detailed visual claims.

**User-observed family constraint:** The user has directly reviewed the broader product family and explicitly classifies the studio plus its product portfolio as a hard “never look like this” reference. That preference is binding even where this research environment cannot independently replay every product.

## D3. Correct engineering conclusion

Do not reverse-engineer or copy proprietary implementation. Instead, use the negative control to review **our own source** for patterns that lead to template sameness:

- one macro marketing-page schema reused everywhere;
- eyebrow/title/description abstraction;
- universal feature cards;
- repeated centered section shell;
- universal fade-up wrapper;
- generic hover-lift language;
- repeated rounded 3-column/4-column grids;
- slogan-fragment copy generation;
- fake/default analytics proof;
- pricing-card grammar leaking into unrelated products;
- multiple product sites sharing one visible macro silhouette.

---

# END — MASTER UI/UX / PRODUCT / VISUAL / CONTENT / CREATIVE FRONTEND BRAIN

This document is the standing design constitution for the Valtum ecosystem. Future product-specific briefs may refine it based on verified product truth and real assets. They may not silently weaken it for implementation convenience.


---

# PHASE 3C SUPPLEMENT — COMPLETE CURRENT RESEARCH, SOURCE AUDIT, VISUAL BLUEPRINT, IMPLEMENTATION ARCHITECTURE, AND ACCEPTANCE STANDARD


## C4. Phase chronology and rejection-learning ledger

The Personality Assessor frontend program is intentionally preserved as a learning sequence rather than rewritten as though the final direction appeared immediately. The mistakes matter because they expose the defaults that future implementation agents must be prevented from reintroducing.

**Phase 0 — research/product truth.** The product was audited as a professional-evidence system rather than a quiz. The initial product truth established the four independent reading families, adaptive assessment, CV/manual context, career relationship engine, development roadmap, longitudinal analytics, AI narrative boundary, privacy controls, and the requirement to distinguish deterministic numeric logic from qualitative AI explanation. This phase also established that competitors are content/category intelligence, not design references.

**Phase 3A — source rebuild.** The implementation removed several obvious historical clichés—legacy gradients, trait preview during questions, fake KPI rows, fake claims, repeated global reveal wrappers—and introduced a cleaner public architecture. The source was materially improved, but the implementation agent was still allowed too much macro-composition freedom. The result became a cleaner template rather than an authored experience.

**Phase 3B — multiple correction passes.** Each correction fixed real source defects, but the process revealed an important failure mode: local source compliance does not guarantee global art direction. Removing one card class while introducing a larger rounded “field,” replacing hover activation with IntersectionObserver state replacement, or converting a 3-column card grid into five stacked framework panels does not solve the underlying composition problem. Phase 3B repeatedly demonstrated why the browser, not the implementation report, must be the final authority.

**Phase 3C — representation-first redesign.** The site is no longer treated as a set of components needing better styling. It is treated as a sequence of product-native visual states whose representations are chosen from media planes, spatial fields, lists, timelines, charts, artifacts, galleries, open typography, illustration, and only occasional true cards. This phase also formalizes a multi-library engineering strategy: choose the correct motion/rendering tool for each interaction rather than forcing all behavior through one library.

The rejection history therefore becomes a permanent design asset. Every future Valtum product should ask not only “what did we approve?” but also “what implementation shortcut produced the rejected result?”| Decision / pattern | Status | Reason / learning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Generic card abundance | REJECTED | The page read as card/card/card/text/text rather than a designed world. Containers were filling design gaps. |
| Stretched horizontal information cards | REJECTED | Changing a card from square to wide does not make it editorial; still container-first filler. |
| Brown/chocolate dark scenes | REJECTED | Felt like food/ice-cream/lifestyle branding rather than calm professional intelligence. |
| Citron/lime as dominant CTA identity | PHASE 3C RECONSIDER | Technically legible, but risks a fashionable “premium tech” fingerprint and competes with image-led color. |
| Factory/machine Pexels RLDjPI-r5fU | REJECTED | Explicit user rejection; do not return to industrial-machine imagery as generic professional evidence. |
| Art director checking monitor xRb2zIFY4_E | REJECTED | Explicit user rejection; generic staged creative-professional monitor scene failed hero threshold. |
| Hero architecture top view Pexels 9618456 | KEEP | User explicitly likes this image; the current failure is placement/scale, not the asset. |
| Hero laptop/professional Pexels 5940721 | KEEP | User explicitly likes this smaller human/professional image; retain as supporting actor. |
| Current generic coding image | REPLACE / RESEARCH | Not perfect; code imagery can remain as a subject only if the photograph has stronger design/evidence context. |
| Five-media hero constellation | REJECTED AS CURRENT COMPOSITION | Too many substantial actors create collision and accidental text occlusion. |
| World 01 / decorative stage labels | REJECTED | Artificial design-system decoration; not meaningful sequence metadata for the visitor. |
| Giant selected card tint / colored edge | REJECTED | Recognized by the user as AI/template visual language. |
| IntersectionObserver image swapping as “flow” | REJECTED FOR FLAGSHIP SCENES | State may technically change, but the transformation can occur outside the useful viewport and lacks continuity/dwell. |
| Desktop pin with oversized 16:10 cards | REJECTED AS CURRENT EXECUTION | The image consumes too much viewport height, hiding the semantic text while scroll is controlled. |
| Desktop grid → 12-column stacked mobile | REJECTED | Responsive but not mobile-designed; violates phone-as-primary-canvas rule. |
| Dashboard universal dashboard-widget architecture | REJECTED AS MACRO SYSTEM | A real dashboard needs hierarchy, charts, lists/tables and varied representations, not universal white cards. |
| Analytics “chart” implemented as table only | REJECTED / INCOMPLETE | Accessible table is useful as a secondary representation, but user explicitly wants actual data visualization. |
| ManyPixels implementation as final illustration style | NOT APPROVED | Illustration category is approved; exact art family and scenes still require a higher quality gate. |
| Generic hover lift/scale/fade-up | REJECTED AS GLOBAL MOTION LANGUAGE | Low-semantic micro-motion is not art direction. |
| Body overflow clipping to hide layout defects | REJECTED | Overflow must be fixed at the responsible actor/geometry; global clipping masks the bug. |


## C5. Current repository truth — main branch audit at the Phase 3C starting point

The current repository is `analyticsmath/PersonalityAnalysis`, branch `main`. The latest inspected redesign commit is `0a83f92b3f6dd64637bc10278216b7941642519f` (“Redesign”). The source is not treated as an enemy: it contains useful infrastructure and real product logic that should be preserved. The redesign objective is to separate **valuable engineering/product truth** from **rejected visible composition**.

### Valuable infrastructure to preserve

- React/Vite routing and existing data hooks.
- React Query data loading/mutation architecture.
- Existing authenticated assessment flow, persistence, CV/manual context, results and career logic.
- The existing responsive media pipeline capable of AVIF/WebP/JPG widths and art-directed mobile sources.
- Real empty/loading/error states and finite-value checks introduced in the source correction passes.
- Accessibility hooks, skip link, keyboard-aware navigation, form semantics, chart summaries/tables where they exist.
- Current deterministic-vs-AI separation and removal of invented numeric fallbacks.
- Existing Recharts dependency for standard chart mechanics where it is appropriate.
- Existing Motion/Framer dependency for application layout/selection transitions.
- Existing Three/R3F infrastructure only where a later product-native visual concept genuinely needs it; it is not a reason to add 3D to Personality.

### Source patterns that currently generate the rejected visual result

The public hero still renders five separate media planes. The dominant architecture photograph is good, but additional wall/developer/scientist/student planes all receive substantial mass and several carry floating labels. The CSS places these actors through percentages (`58%`, `22%`, `20%`, etc.) without enough height-aware collision control. This is why an asset set that looks good individually still produces a crowded opening composition.

The Work Worlds theatre currently makes each panel roughly `58vw` wide with a fixed `16 / 10` media ratio. On a 1440×900 or especially a 1366×768 viewport, width-driven height consumes the active stage. The copy then falls beneath the most valuable viewport region even though the scene is pinned. Phase 3C will use a **dual constraint**: both inline width and block-height envelope govern the active photograph.

The public implementation still contains tag-like labels and stage-label styles such as `work-world-panel__tag` and `theatre-stage-tag`, which conflict with the no-eyebrow/no-decorative-stage-label constitution. Several later scenes continue to use large white bounded surfaces and “pills” under different class names. Renaming them did not solve the underlying representation problem.

The secondary public routes use IntersectionObserver to replace the active image or stage content as the user scrolls. IntersectionObserver is excellent for visibility/state detection, but it does not automatically produce continuity. In the current use, the user can cross a threshold while the actor is not centrally established, so the visible change is weak or missed. Flagship route sequences will become true persistent-state scenes, while ordinary route chapters may still use observer-driven activation when continuity is not required.

The authenticated product shell establishes a 12-column grid and a universal `.dashboard-widget` card with white background, border, radius, padding and subtle shadow. Below 1120 px, most spans become `12`, which effectively means the desktop composition stacks vertically. This source architecture directly explains the user’s “dashboard was destroyed” reaction: it made the application structurally responsive but did not give mobile a distinct information hierarchy.

The analytics page calls a component `TraitTrendChart`, but the current component renders an accessible table rather than a visual trend chart. The table is valuable as an accessibility/data-detail alternative; the Phase 3C dashboard must add the actual line/small-multiple visualization rather than deleting the table.

The analytics overview and career-readiness components still rely heavily on repeated `MetricCard` primitives. Even when values are truthful, six equal metric cards flatten hierarchy and recreate the very dashboard skin the user rejected. The information must be redistributed into a dominant profile state, trend/relationship visuals, compact status rows, and ranked/structured objects.

The assessment CSS currently presents Likert choices as five large rectangular option surfaces and MCQ choices as rounded cards. On mobile, the Likert surfaces simply become a vertical list. The assessment should remain operational and calm, but it can be far more compact: one question owns the viewport, a horizontal segmented/anchored scale where appropriate on desktop, and 52–60 px touch rows on mobile.

A concrete runtime defect exists in the current auth source: the Login and Signup pages dereference `publicMedia.auth.login` and `publicMedia.auth.signup`, while the current exported media registry contains no `auth` object. That is a source-level exception candidate and plausibly explains blank auth routes. Phase 3C must repair the data contract before judging the final auth composition.

Finally, the public motion root currently instantiates GSAP ScrollSmoother globally on fine-pointer desktop. Phase 3C will test replacing this with a lighter native-scroll-compatible Lenis layer while preserving native scrolling as the source of truth. ScrollSmoother and Lenis must never run simultaneously.
## C6. Product truth and evidence boundaries that visual design may not override

Personality Assessor combines multiple independent professional interpretation systems. The design must never visually imply that one framework is “the truth” while another is supporting decoration. Big Five dimensions, RIASEC interests, Work Values and Career Signals should remain visibly distinct readings of the same evidence. The user’s professional context, answers, behavioral prompts and stored evidence may contribute to interpretation, but a front-end animation is not allowed to invent causal precision the backend does not provide.

The scoring architecture is deterministic for core numeric dimensions and career-fit logic; AI provides qualitative narrative/interpretive assistance and cannot silently replace the underlying scores. Therefore, numeric visualizations should come from real deterministic values, while AI-generated prose should be visually identified through provenance/status rather than blended into the same visual encoding as deterministic measures.

Career-fit weights documented in the project source are currently RIASEC 25%, skill 25%, Work Values 20%, Big Five 15%, education/context 10%, and goal/domain 5%. These weights can inform a fit-breakdown visualization if and only if the live endpoint being rendered actually returns the relevant component values. The design must not reverse-engineer a fictional component breakdown from a single total score.

The privacy/retention area still contains a source divergence: one architectural/privacy interpretation says records remain until user deletion/default no automatic purge, while the AssessmentSession source has TTL behavior for in-progress and completed session documents. Public retention copy must remain conservative until this is reconciled. The visual design may make controls clearer, but it cannot resolve a legal/data-policy ambiguity by styling it away.

Likewise, the exact canonical “question count” should not become permanent marketing copy until the live adaptive journey is verified. Historical implementation material contains a bank of variants and screenshots showing runtime counts such as 22, while conversational descriptions have mentioned another count. A premium site tells the truth even when a simpler marketing number would be convenient.
## C7. Phase 3C reference synthesis — principles, not surfaces

| Reference intelligence | Transferable principle | Surface explicitly not transferred |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Oryzo / Lusion BTS | Continuity is more valuable than isolated flashy sections; product/protagonist remains central; believable world beats abstract Awwwards styling; pipeline follows the idea; UI becomes quieter when the world is already expressive. | Do not copy coaster, desk, satire, exact 3D, colors, or humour. |
| White Desert | Environment persists across content; media controls rhythm; image color can carry the brand; foreground content changes while world remains coherent; footer can resolve as final scene. | Do not copy Antarctica, ice palette, camp geometry, coordinates. |
| Dogstudio — Zoox | A real product shape can generate frames/buttons/composition; concept should be discovered before implementation; 3D/media only when it explains the product. | Do not import robotaxi styling or WebGL for its own sake. |
| Dogstudio — Virgin Galactic | Immersive and content-heavy chapters can be bridged with transitions; WebGL subscenes require deliberate transition/performance planning; frontend/rendering must communicate. | Personality does not need spacecraft-scale WebGL; transfer transition planning and performance discipline. |
| Glyphic reference | Typography, media and central visual can inhabit one field instead of two-column hero. | Do not copy exact type/organic object/layout. |
| User’s 20-reference board | Dominant media, partial neighbors, unequal scale, environmental fields, collage/orbit/rail, image-type interlock, persistent background, varied scene pressure. | Do not turn the board into a template inventory. |
| Dashboard reference boards | Real dashboards use hierarchy, charts, lists/tables, calendars, imagery/illustration, asymmetry and semantic color. | Do not copy fashionable skins, fake data, or unrelated KPI layouts. |
| Character illustration references | Flat editorial people with purposeful objects, restrained palette, coherent proportions, transparent background, professional context. | Do not mix unrelated illustration families or use generic mascot blobs. |

### The updated anti-copy test

For every idea borrowed from a reference, remove the reference name and ask whether the decision still follows from Personality Assessor’s product truth. If the answer is no, the idea is decorative borrowing. If the answer is yes—because professional evidence really does transform, because career environments really are a browsable collection, because the profile really has four independent readings, because historical assessments really form a time series—then the reference has taught a design principle rather than a surface.

The same standard applies to technology. A site using GSAP, Lenis, Rive, Motion and WebGL is not inherently more advanced than a site using CSS and semantic HTML. High-end studios repeatedly demonstrate that the difficult work is transition planning, asset preparation, responsive composition, state management and performance discipline. Phase 3C therefore uses a **technology ownership map** rather than a “use all modern libraries” checklist.
## C8. Technology research and ownership matrix

| Technology | Phase 3C role | Owned interaction domain | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Native browser scroll | Source of truth / fallback | Every public and authenticated route | Keep semantics, anchor behavior, keyboard, restoration and reduced-motion coherent even without enhancement. |
| Lenis | Public marketing scroll smoothing on desktop/fine pointer after QA | Marketing pages only | Lightweight/native-scroll compatible. Do not run on touch by default. Do not coexist with ScrollSmoother. |
| GSAP ScrollTrigger | Flagship pinned scenic timelines | Work Worlds; Context→Question→Signal; Living Profile; cross-scene actor carries | Precise pin/scrub/snap/velocity/reverse. Pin stable stage; animate nested contents, not pinned root. |
| Anime.js v4 | Optional isolated timeline/SVG/type sequence | Only where a local effect is simpler than GSAP/Motion | Valid scroll Observer/timeline system, but do not make two libraries fight over the same flagship scroll timeline. |
| Motion for React | Authenticated UI layout/state continuity | tabs, list→detail, selection, dialogs, career explorer, dashboard rearrangement | Interruptible layout/shared-element animation is more appropriate than a theatrical scroll engine in product UI. |
| View Transition API / React Router integration | Progressive route/shared-element transitions | career list→detail, result↔explorer, selected marketing/detail images where safe | Native snapshot transitions degrade gracefully; avoid complex overlapping full-page choreography where scroll deltas make them awkward. |
| CSS Scroll-driven Animations | Progressive noncritical effects | read-progress, subtle local parallax, low-risk reveal/progress cues | Do not put core story behind limited-availability features without fallback. |
| Embla Carousel | Optional advanced touch gallery engine | Only if native snap cannot satisfy Work Worlds/role-gallery accessibility and control requirements | Supports variable widths, drag-free, axis, tween effects. Prefer native scroll snap first. |
| Rive | Optional authored vector illustration animation | Only if approved illustration assets are designed for interactive state | Useful state machines, but runtime/WASM cost means static SVG remains the default. Do not add Rive merely to animate stock illustrations. |
| Recharts | Standard dashboard chart mechanics | line/radar/bar where standard charting is semantically correct | Heavily art-direct; no default Recharts skin. Bespoke SVG/DOM for lollipop/relationship views when simpler. |
| Three / R3F | Reserved | No core Phase 3C Personality scene currently requires it | Existing dependency is not justification for 3D. Add a realtime renderer only when a product-native idea cannot be preserved more cheaply. |
| @barba/core | Remove/avoid in this React Router SPA | None | A second navigation lifecycle creates unnecessary complexity. React Router/View Transitions/Motion already cover route needs. |
| GSAP ScrollSmoother | Phase out if Lenis passes validation | Currently public global root | Do not stack smoothing engines. Preserve native fallback. |

### Why multiple libraries are acceptable but multiple owners are not

The user’s request to understand Anime.js and other technologies is correct: an expert frontend architect should know more than one tool. The wrong conclusion would be to use multiple libraries in the same interaction merely to prove sophistication. Phase 3C uses **one owner per motion problem**. GSAP owns pinned narrative scroll timelines. Motion owns React layout/selection continuity. Lenis, if accepted after QA, owns desktop scroll interpolation. Native scroll snap owns straightforward touch rails. Rive may own a self-contained vector illustration state machine. Recharts may own chart scales/paths for standard analytical plots. This separation reduces event-loop fights, transform collisions, double smoothing, cleanup bugs and accessibility regressions.

### Behind-the-scenes design-engineering lesson

Published studio BTS material repeatedly shows that elite execution begins before code. Lusion’s Oryzo pipeline selected Houdini/Redshift/scanning/real-time techniques after the concept demanded them. Dogstudio’s case studies discuss planning WebGL subscenes, render loops, asset optimization, interaction events and transitions as one system. The transferable lesson is not “use WebGL.” It is **art direction and technical architecture must be solved together at scene boundaries**. Personality Assessor’s equivalent is much lighter: DOM, responsive photography, SVG/data visualization, controlled scroll timelines and vector illustration are sufficient for the current thesis.

## C9. Color system research and Phase 3C decision gate

The color system must stop carrying more identity weight than the product needs. The strongest reference sites often let photography or the product world supply chromatic richness while controls remain restrained. Platform guidance similarly treats color as a semantic/continuity tool rather than something every control needs.

The Phase 3B citron accent solved contrast but created a new risk: it became a visible “premium tech” signature, especially when used as a large CTA fill in the header. Because the Brain already warns against green-led premium-tech reflexes, Phase 3C does **not** automatically preserve citron as the brand event.

### Phase 3C palette candidate — Mineral Neutral + small warm marker

This remains **PENDING USER APPROVAL** until it is reviewed in rendered hero, dashboard and mobile frames.

| Role | Candidate | Intended behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Canvas | `#F6F8F7` | Cool-neutral background; not beige/cream. |
| Paper | `#FFFFFF` | Forms/data surfaces when a surface is semantically needed. |
| Primary ink | `#101313` | Main type, navigation, primary CTA fill. |
| Deep secondary | `#2B3230` | Dense UI/data hierarchy. |
| Secondary text | `#596360` | Explanatory copy. |
| Mist | `#DDE3E1` | Quiet boundaries, tracks, separators only where functional. |
| Soft field | `#EEF2F0` | Input/chart tracks/quiet selected background where meaning requires. |
| Dark field | `#101615` | Limited theatrical/media scenes such as Work Worlds; not a page-wide identity. |
| Dark foreground | `#F6F8F7` | Dark-field text. |
| Dark muted | `#BBC4C1` | Subordinate dark-field copy. |
| Identity marker candidate | `#F2B84B` | Very small 3–6% warm marker for active state/data event, not giant CTA panels. |
| Info | `#315E8A` | Semantic information status. |
| Success | `#1E6B50` | Semantic success only. |
| Warning | `#9A630F` | Semantic warning only. |
| Error | `#A33A45` | Errors/destructive state. |

An equally valid Phase 3C option is **no marketing accent at all**: use ink/paper UI and allow the photography to carry color, reserving semantic colors for the application. This option may ultimately feel more authored and less trend-dependent. The final palette decision must be judged in three simultaneous contexts: hero with the architecture image, a populated analytical dashboard, and a 390px assessment screen. A palette that only makes the homepage look good is not sufficient.

### Color prohibitions

Do not create hierarchy by tinting every module differently. Do not encode Big Five/RIASEC/Work Values with a permanent rainbow taxonomy. Do not reuse the same accent to mean brand, selected state, success and high score. Chart color is data semantics, not decoration. All critical state must survive monochrome and must not rely on hue alone.

## C10. Typography system — Phase 3C role architecture

Mona Sans 2 remains the strongest current single-family candidate because its variable weight, width and optical-size axes can support spatial marketing type, calm reading, dense UI and tabular data without introducing an arbitrary “luxury” companion. It is **not** accepted because it is trendy; it remains accepted as the current technical candidate because the family has the axes needed to make one identity behave differently across contexts.

### Recommended role ranges

- **Hero/spatial display:** weight approximately 430–560; width 107–118% when the composition benefits; optical size set for display; avoid synthetic extra-bold authority. Desktop line breaks are explicitly authored.
- **Major editorial route titles:** 480–600; width 100–112%; display optical size; paragraph measure remains restrained.
- **Reading/body:** 400–450; width 100%; text optical size; line height typically 1.45–1.62 depending measure.
- **Application UI:** 450–550; compact but not cramped; labels communicate hierarchy through size/position before weight.
- **Data/numerals:** tabular numerals where comparison matters; labels must not rely on all-caps microcopy.
- **Mobile display:** separate sizes and breaks rather than proportional shrinking of desktop.

### Typography anti-template rules

The phrase “large centered heading” is not a composition. A major heading earns scale when it controls the scene, interacts with media or creates pacing. Do not center every section title. Do not use all-caps utility labels as decorative eyebrows. Do not tighten tracking until letterforms collide simply to mimic contemporary portfolio work. Do not place 60–90 character lines of body text across a wide desktop. Do not let the browser randomly create the hero line breaks after the art direction is approved.

## C11. Spatial sizing, spacing and image-shape doctrine

The user’s most repeated Phase 3B criticism was not “the image is wrong” but **the image is the wrong size, in the wrong place, with the wrong amount of surrounding space**. Phase 3C treats geometry as a first-class design specification.

### Dual-axis sizing

A media actor in a pinned scene receives both an inline and block constraint. For example, Work Worlds may allow an active image around `50–58vw`, but it also receives a block envelope around `52–58svh`. Whichever constraint becomes critical wins. This prevents a 16:10 photograph from growing so tall that its explanation disappears below the viewport.

### Viewport-sensitive scene fit

Every pinned desktop stage must be validated at least at 1440×900 and 1366×768. A composition that only works on a tall designer monitor is rejected. Header height, stage top/bottom breathing room, state controls, media and explanatory copy must fit together without relying on the user to finish the pin before reading the content.

### Gutters and macro spacing

Indicative gutters, not universal templates:

- 1440px desktop: 48–64px outer field depending scene pressure.
- 1024px: 32–40px.
- 768px: 24–32px.
- 430/390/375/360/320px: approximately 18–20px, with edge-to-edge media allowed when the composition earns it.

A useful micro scale remains `4 / 8 / 12 / 16 / 24 / 32 / 48 / 64 / 96 / 128`, but macro spacing is scene rhythm. Repeating `padding-block: 96px` on every section recreates a template even if the token is well chosen.

### Image shapes

Image shape comes from subject and role:

- environment/protagonist: landscape or subject-native crop;
- human/professional supporting actor: portrait/square only if face/hands/work object remain meaningful;
- artifact detail: can be narrow/tall/irregular if that is how the object reads;
- neighboring gallery context: partial clipping is allowed and often useful;
- cards are not required to create a crop. An image plane may be bounded by a mask without becoming a “card.”

No universal `border-radius: 20px` image language. Some images may be almost square-cornered; some may use a controlled 10–18px radius; an editorial mask can be asymmetric if it belongs to the scene. The shape must not become a signature copied to every project.

### Crop contract

Every important image gets an explicit desktop crop, tablet crop, mobile source/crop, focal point and `object-position`. The existing responsive media component already supports separate mobile sources, so “the crop is bad on phone” is a design failure, not an infrastructure excuse.

## C12. Card and container semantics — the definitive Phase 3C rule

A card is permitted when the user perceives the object as independent: a selectable career, a saved assessment record, an upload file, a notification, an invoice-like record, a discrete status/action object, or a dashboard entity that can be scanned/selected independently. A card is rejected when its only purpose is to wrap a section heading and paragraph, create artificial visual weight, or fill a grid.

### Representation mapping

| Information behavior | Default representation |
|---|---|
| One environment / profession / work context | media plane or environment field |
| A browsable sequence of worlds | authored rail/gallery with active/neighbor states |
| Explanatory narrative | open typography + spatial anchor |
| Repeated compact facts | structured rows/list |
| Dense comparable records | table |
| Trend over time | line chart / small multiple |
| Ranked dimensions | lollipop or horizontal bar |
| Relationship among dimensions | radar/relationship map plus precise labels |
| Evidence provenance | artifact/field/timeline depending structure |
| Process/transformation | persistent state theatre / timeline |
| Independent selectable role | true card/row allowed |
| User action requiring confirmation | dialog/surface allowed |
| Empty state | illustration + direct action + concise explanation |

The implementation agent may not convert this map back into a universal `Card` macro for convenience. Low-level border/radius primitives can be reused, but page architecture must remain authored.

## C13. Motion grammar — from threshold swaps to visible state theatre

The defining Phase 3C motion correction is **viewport ownership**. A meaningful transformation is not successful because the JavaScript state changed; it is successful when the visitor can see the actor before, during and after the change.

### Required flagship timeline grammar

> **enter → establish → transform → dwell → carry → resolve → release**

“Establish” means the primary actor is stably visible near the intended focal zone before transformation begins. “Dwell” means the state remains legible for a meaningful progress interval after the transformation lands. “Carry” means a product-native actor or visual relationship can cross a scene boundary where continuity is beneficial. “Release” returns control to normal document flow without a hard snap.

A normalized multi-state sequence may allocate progress approximately as:

- 0.00–0.08: enter / establish
- 0.08–0.12: breathing threshold
- 0.12–0.27: A → B
- 0.27–0.34: B dwell
- 0.34–0.50: B → C
- 0.50–0.58: C dwell
- 0.58–0.75: C → D
- 0.75–0.84: D dwell
- 0.84–0.94: resolve / carry
- 0.94–1.00: release preparation

The exact values are scene-specific; the mandatory principle is **dwell exists**.

### Motion vocabulary

The Brain’s motion vocabulary remains: **Reveal, Carry, Exchange, Pin, Rail, Inspect, Resolve, Route.** Phase 3C adds a practical distinction between *continuous* and *discrete* state changes. Continuous state theatre should move/scale/crop/occlude/reconfigure persistent actors; discrete React state swaps are reserved for ordinary UI where cinematic continuity is unnecessary.

### Reverse behavior

Every pinned timeline is tested forward and backward. No one-way cleanup assumption. A carried actor must return to its source; selected profile states must reverse coherently; previous/next navigation must map to the same timeline state as scroll; scroll oscillation around a boundary must not flicker the DOM or leave duplicated fixed proxies.

### Reduced motion

Reduced-motion is not “duration 0 on the same pin.” Pinned scroll-jacking should collapse into readable static chapters or direct controls. The semantic order remains the same, but the user is never forced through long scroll distances to reveal content that could be present immediately.

## C14. Public homepage — exact Phase 3C visual blueprint


### Scene 1 — Opening Evidence Field

**Scene job:** Establish the product proposition and show professional evidence as a tangible world before introducing the assessment mechanics.

**Primary actors:** Dominant architecture top-view photograph (Pexels 9618456), retained professional/laptop supporting photograph (Pexels 5940721), and at most one subtle evidence/process fragment. Headline and CTA are actors too.

**Desktop composition:** At 1440×900, the architecture photograph owns roughly 50–58% of the useful visual mass but is bounded by height. It begins right-of-center and slightly above the optical midline, leaving an intentional quiet region for the headline. The supporting human/laptop photograph is much smaller—approximately 15–20% mass—and positioned to create depth without blocking the proposition. One process fragment may partially clip from an edge. The header belongs to this field rather than sitting in a detached pill.

**Mobile composition:** At 390px, do not show a horizontal carousel of four giant 78vw cards. Use a portrait art-directed crop of the architecture image as the main visual, then one smaller human/professional fragment that overlaps or follows with deliberate breathing room. The headline receives mobile-specific line breaks. CTA and “how it works” remain thumb-accessible without competing with media.

**Motion/state behavior:** LCP media is visible immediately. Headline can reveal through restrained masks, but the media does not wait behind opacity. On scroll, nonessential fragment settles/recedes; one evidence cue may carry toward Work Worlds. No five-plane choreography.

**Content rule:** Working proposition remains “Your work leaves evidence.” until final content approval. Supporting copy should explain professional context + adaptive responses + inspectable multidimensional profile in one concise paragraph.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No floating image labels, no generic code photo unless replaced by a better art-directed evidence image, no giant lime CTA, no text running through the most detailed portion of the architecture drawing, no equal collage.

### Scene 2 — Work Worlds Pinned Media Theatre

**Scene job:** Demonstrate that professional behavior reveals itself differently across different kinds of work, while giving the homepage its one major horizontal spatial sequence.

**Primary actors:** Six real work-world photographs: Build, Investigate, Make, Shape, Structure, Collaborate. Active media is protagonist; previous/next are context. Short semantic copy remains visible during the state.

**Desktop composition:** Full stage is pinned. Header/title, active media, concise world statement and navigation/state indicator all fit within one viewport. Active media approximately 50–58vw but max 52–58svh. Previous image is a partial sliver; next image is a visible preview. The active panel is not a giant card: media and typography share a stage.

**Mobile composition:** Native touch gallery or Embla only if native snap proves insufficient. One active image around 78–86vw with a meaningful next peek, compact title/copy under or integrated with the image, direct dots/labels that remain accessible. No desktop pin on touch.

**Motion/state behavior:** Vertical input advances the horizontal rail while the stage remains pinned. State transitions include dwell. Direction reverses naturally. Direct Prev/Next buttons map to exact timeline labels rather than independent React state. Image parallax/scale may be very subtle and only if it reinforces depth.

**Content rule:** Remove “World 01” and other decorative sequence labels. Use the actual world name and a one-sentence interpretation of what that environment reveals.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No oversized 16:10 frame hiding copy, no opacity-only “active” state, no invisible text below pin, no six equal image cards, no hover zoom as primary motion.

### Scene 3 — Quiet Bridge / Evidence Compression

**Scene job:** Release visual pressure after Work Worlds and prepare the visitor for the core interpretation sequence.

**Primary actors:** A small carried evidence fragment, one sentence, and perhaps a simplified CV/profile artifact edge.

**Desktop composition:** Open field, comparatively little chrome. The page should feel as if the broad world of professional environments is compressing toward one person’s evidence. Avoid enclosing the bridge in a section card.

**Mobile composition:** Short, readable transition with one visual cue; no extra scroll theatre.

**Motion/state behavior:** The last Work World resolves; one fragment or alignment cue becomes smaller/more document-like and leads the eye toward the next pinned scene.

**Content rule:** The conceptual message is that context changes what a useful question should ask.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No centered H2 + paragraph + three cards; no decorative divider line.

### Scene 4 — Context → Question → Signal Flagship Theatre

**Scene job:** Make the product’s adaptive logic understandable without claiming causal precision that the backend does not expose.

**Primary actors:** One professional-context/CV artifact, five real evidence categories, one selected evidence anchor, one adaptive question, response options, one resulting observed strategy signal.

**Desktop composition:** A stable pinned stage owns ~100svh. The document/artifact starts as the main actor at 60–72% of usable stage. Evidence annotations emerge around/within it without becoming five equal cards. As context narrows, irrelevant evidence quiets and one anchor remains. The question grows from the anchor; response choices appear in the same coordinate system. The chosen response compresses to a signal marker that will carry into the next scene.

**Mobile composition:** No long pin. Break into direct states: context summary → highlighted evidence → question → response → signal. Each state can occupy a phone viewport; user scroll/tap continues naturally. Preserve the conceptual carry with a small persistent marker rather than scroll-jacking.

**Motion/state behavior:** Persistent DOM actors. Use GSAP timeline with labels and dwell. Avoid `display:none` swaps at thresholds. Responses may exchange/slide/reorder but not bounce. Manual selection overrides automatic demo state and timeline settles around the user’s choice.

**Content rule:** Public demo must be marked as illustrative. It must never imply that one CV line deterministically generated a psychometric conclusion.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No giant rounded document card, no five rounded evidence cards, no “Targeting anchor” badge, no selected option with arbitrary colored edge, no state change offscreen.

### Scene 5 — Living Multidimensional Profile

**Scene job:** Show that one evidence stream produces four independent readings rather than one personality type.

**Primary actors:** The carried signal/evidence marker plus four representation systems: Big Five lollipop positions, RIASEC relationship overview, Work Values ranking, Career Signals capability/evidence field.

**Desktop composition:** The stage stays pinned long enough for all four states to be centrally established. The central profile object persists while the representation changes. A compact direct-access control allows keyboard/pointer navigation but does not become a row of giant pills. Data visual area occupies the majority of stage; explanatory text remains concise.

**Mobile composition:** One lens owns the screen at a time. Use swipe/tabs/select controls with explicit labels. The chart/representation has a touch-friendly inspection state and direct textual summary.

**Motion/state behavior:** Profile representation morphs continuously where possible: bars condense/rearrange, signal points reposition, labels exchange. Do not dissolve the entire component and mount a new card. Scroll labels and direct controls map to the same state machine.

**Content rule:** “One profile. Four distinct readings.” remains a strong working thesis. Explain independence rather than ranking one framework above another.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No rainbow taxonomy, no giant white container with tab buttons above it, no permanent first-tab selected design as the main composition, no 3D trait sphere.

### Scene 6 — Evidence & Confidence Inspection Field

**Scene job:** Teach the user that evidence can be strong, mixed, or limited and that confidence is contextual metadata—not truth probability.

**Primary actors:** Evidence points/rows, interpretation relationships, explicit missing-context space, provenance/status language.

**Desktop composition:** Open analytical field. Strong evidence may appear as denser linked artifacts; mixed evidence as competing or separated relationships; missing context is represented through literal sparsity/empty area and text, not a dotted-border card. A methodology link is integrated near the boundary explanation.

**Mobile composition:** Three stacked semantic chapters are allowed, but avoid card shells. Use spacing, headings, symbols and actual evidence lists.

**Motion/state behavior:** Subtle Inspect behavior: selecting evidence highlights its relation/provenance. No theatrical scroll pin required unless the final data proves a compelling sequence.

**Content rule:** Never call confidence a probability of truth. Never publish internal-consistency/scientific-validation claims unless documented.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No three equal tinted cards, no “Strong Signal” green top border as generic hierarchy, no vanity confidence badge.

### Scene 7 — Career Relationships

**Scene job:** Reconnect the analytical profile to real professional environments and explain why a role relates, where the stretch is, and what could strengthen fit.

**Primary actors:** Compact role index, one active professional/environment photograph, one fit breakdown visualization, open reasoning typography.

**Desktop composition:** Asymmetric master-detail. Active environment/media receives ~55–62% of primary stage. Role list is compact and scannable rather than seven giant cards. Reasoning sits in open editorial blocks adjacent/below the active media. A small breakdown chart can show component relationships only if real component values exist.

**Mobile composition:** Role list first; selecting a role moves to a dedicated detail state/page with Back to roles. Use Motion/View Transition for continuity if supported. Image receives an art-directed mobile crop.

**Motion/state behavior:** Active media and reasoning exchange smoothly on selection. Shared-element/layout animation is appropriate; no scroll-driven carousel required.

**Content rule:** Fit is guidance, not a verdict. Public sample values remain clearly illustrative.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No full-height list of equal 88/84/81% cards, no giant generic coding photo for every technical career, no three stacked reasoning cards.

### Scene 8 — Development Loop / Human Illustration

**Scene job:** Translate a static result into a human development cycle: gap → deliberate action → visible work → new evidence → profile update.

**Primary actors:** One approved editorial workplace illustration, one moving evidence/artifact token, compact step labels.

**Desktop composition:** Illustration and process share one composition. The figure/team should not sit inside a generic white card. Steps can arc, route or flow around the illustration depending approved artwork. The active step is indicated by proximity/position/small marker, not a colored border on a long card.

**Mobile composition:** Vertical step sequence around a cropped illustration or separate lightweight illustration states. Keep the next action obvious.

**Motion/state behavior:** The evidence token moves through the loop; the illustration may have minimal local motion if Rive/custom SVG is approved, but static SVG plus DOM motion is sufficient by default.

**Content rule:** Focus on tangible work: project, artifact, feedback, updated context. Avoid motivational slogans.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No four long horizontal cards beside a stock illustration; no decorative numbered steps; no unrelated generic teamwork scene.

### Scene 9 — Trust / Boundary Release

**Scene job:** Lower visual pressure and state the deterministic/AI/privacy boundaries factually before the close.

**Primary actors:** Typography, small logic diagram, provenance/state icons only when informative.

**Desktop composition:** Quiet open field, short sections, one simple deterministic→AI narrative diagram if it adds clarity. Avoid a giant trust card grid.

**Mobile composition:** Plain readable chapters with direct links to methodology/privacy.

**Motion/state behavior:** Minimal. A small line/relationship reveal can occur, but trust should not become spectacle.

**Content rule:** AI narrates/assists; deterministic scores remain independent. Privacy claims remain conservative where retention divergence is unresolved.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No “We believe in total transparency,” no certification badges without proof, no glowing AI boundary graphic.

### Scene 10 — Terminal Footer

**Scene job:** Resolve the journey with a memorable final identity statement and useful navigation.

**Primary actors:** Large terminal typography, concise final proposition, utility links, privacy/methodology/sign-in.

**Desktop composition:** Utility content can sit above/around the terminal word/phrase; final typography can become extremely large but must not use decorative vertical/horizontal grid lines from the reference. Dark or light ending is decided by the preceding scene and contrast; dark is not mandatory.

**Mobile composition:** Large type still feels deliberate but does not create horizontal overflow. Utility links are touch-friendly and grouped by task.

**Motion/state behavior:** A modest final settle/reveal is enough. No forced scroll lock.

**Content rule:** “Bring new evidence back.” is a strong working close if retained after copy review.

**Acceptance / rejection guard:** No generic four-column SaaS footer, no decorative guide lines, no brand word so large it clips accidentally rather than intentionally.

## C15. Secondary public routes — each route gets a different macro-composition

| Route | Phase 3C macro concept | Explicitly reject |
| --- | --- | --- |
| How It Works | One evidence artifact travels through intake, parsing, adaptive question, four readings and career relationship. Use a persistent visual canvas on desktop with adjacent narrative checkpoints; on mobile each checkpoint becomes a direct full-width state. | Do not keep the current four text articles whose only visual change is swapping an image at an IntersectionObserver threshold. |
| Career Intelligence | A career atlas/master-detail environment. Profession index is compact; active profession is large media plus relationship breakdown and “why / stretch / strengthen” explanation. Include a role-search/explore affordance only if the real product supports it. | Do not reproduce the homepage career scene at a different scale; this route must support deeper comparison/exploration. |
| Progress | A longitudinal loop: current gap → planned action → visible artifact → completed evidence → profile change. Use a timeline/route with illustration and artifact evidence. If history exists, connect to real progress records. | Do not render six large horizontal stages or observer-driven photo swaps. |
| Methodology | Research-book/atlas composition. Big Five continuous dimensions, RIASEC, Work Values, Career Signals and deterministic/AI boundary each get the representation appropriate to the concept: spectrum, relationship diagram, ranked hierarchy, evidence map, logic boundary. | Do not stack five giant white rectangles. Do not use decorative tags or unsupported “validated/calibrated” claims. |
| Trust | Logic ledger/boundary map. Show what is deterministic, what AI may narrate, what metadata means, how missing evidence is represented, and what the system does not claim. | Do not use four equal trust cards, certification-like iconography, or emotional marketing copy. |
| Privacy | Data lifecycle/control map: profile/CV source, export, selective assessment deletion, account deletion, AI processing boundary. Use settings rows/tables/actions where operational. | Do not publish unresolved retention promises. Do not make destructive actions visually playful. |
| Login | Focused return gateway. Form is the task protagonist; one art-directed professional evidence fragment supplies identity. Keep navigation minimal. | First repair `publicMedia.auth` source mismatch. Do not make login an empty page or duplicate the marketing hero. |
| Signup | Intentional beginning state. Form + concise “what happens next” context + one evidence-oriented visual. Terms/consent are explicit. | Do not duplicate login with one extra input. Mobile is form-first; visual becomes secondary or collapses. |
| 404 / error | Product-consistent recovery state with direct routes home/sign-in/dashboard as appropriate. Illustration optional only if it communicates recovery. | No jokey generic 404 mascot that breaks the professional tone. |


## C16. Assessment experience — focused operational composition

The assessment is the point at which “award-site” instincts must be constrained. The user is answering questions, not watching a show. The goal is calm focus, speed, clarity, recoverability and truthful progress.

### Setup / onboarding

**Role state.** Student, graduate and professional are real options and may be rendered as selectable objects, but they should not become three enormous decorative cards. Use compact choice rows/segments with concise consequence copy. If an illustration is used, one scene can visually locate the choice without forcing each role into its own illustration tile.

**CV/manual context state.** The upload zone is allowed to have a boundary because drag/drop is a real interactive region. Its boundary should feel functional rather than like another feature card. Parsing state must use real loading/progress where possible; never a fake timer. Manual entry remains an equally legitimate route.

**Review state.** Parsed context is better represented as a structured document/artifact/list with editable groups than a giant rounded card filled with tags. Skills/experience/education/project items should read like inspectable evidence.

### Question screen

One question owns the moment. The quiet header contains brand/home escape, progress (`Question X of Y` only if Y is actually known at runtime), autosave status and Save/Exit. Back is always safe.

Question typography should respond to length. A short question can be 38–44px on desktop; a long domain-specific scenario may step down toward 30–36px to remain readable without pushing answers below the fold. Mobile roughly 26–34px with a readable measure.

**Likert:** on desktop, prefer one compact 5-position segmented/anchored scale or a row of equally reachable choice cells around 64–72px tall. They are controls, so a bounded hit target is correct; they should not look like five marketing cards. Active state uses strong outline/ink/marker and explicit label, not a giant purple/green tinted slab. On phone, five 52–60px rows or an accessible compact scale is acceptable, depending label length.

**MCQ/scenario:** 56–72px rows with letter/index marker only if useful, full-row tap target, visible selected state, no hover dependency. Longer answers can wrap, but row density should remain controlled.

**Behavior prompt:** one real-example prompt, calm textarea, character requirement stated literally, clear continue/back. No cinematic animation between every keystroke/state.

### Question transitions

Motion is quick and functional: outgoing question may shift/fade slightly while incoming content enters from the logical forward direction; Back reverses direction. Avoid springs, bounce and long fades. Transition duration should generally live around 180–320ms and remain interruptible. Reduced motion swaps directly with focus management.

## C17. Authenticated product shell — dashboard-first, not marketing-inside-an-app

The product workspace needs to feel like a real application. It can share typography and identity with marketing, but its visual pressure is different: information density rises, motion becomes more functional, and card semantics are stricter.

### Desktop shell

A 216–232px sidebar remains a reasonable candidate if the final route inventory supports persistent navigation. It should be visually quiet, with one clear active state. Avoid a huge contrasting pill for every active link. The top utility bar should not repeat the page title and actions if the page itself already establishes them more effectively; the shell can become lighter and let the content own hierarchy.

The workspace should generally cap around 1360–1440px of readable/analytical width while allowing certain charts/tables to expand. Outer gutters should reduce compared with the marketing site. Dense data screens should not inherit cinematic 96–128px vertical gaps.

### Mobile shell

The existing bottom navigation concept is viable, but Phase 3C treats phone task priority explicitly: Overview, Analytics, Assess/Resume, and Account/Privacy are enough; deeper sections use in-page navigation. The bottom bar must reserve safe-area inset, avoid covering content, and keep each target approximately 44–48px or larger.

A mobile dashboard is **not** all desktop widgets in one column. It is a re-ordered task flow: current state/next action → profile snapshot → one analytical relationship → career direction → history/utility. Secondary visualizations move to dedicated views or horizontally inspectable modules when necessary.

## C18. Dashboard — zero-data and populated blueprints

### Zero-data dashboard

Zero data is a real product state, not an unfinished dashboard. The top of the page should show the person’s identity/context state, `0 completed assessments` as a truthful count, one strong editorial workplace illustration, and the single most important action: start the first assessment / provide professional context. A short “what becomes available” diagram can preview the four future reading families without showing fabricated scores.

If the user already has an active incomplete session, that state supersedes the generic zero-data welcome. Resume is the primary action; “start another assessment” should not compete unless the product permits it.

Do not render empty axes, fake line charts or `0%` trait bars. A zero count is valid. An unmeasured trait is “not available.”

### Populated dashboard hierarchy

The populated dashboard is not a grid of equal widgets. A recommended attention hierarchy is:

1. **Current profile / next action zone** — dominant. Shows latest assessment date/status, concise profile summary, active/incomplete state and next development action.
2. **Profile readings zone** — Big Five lollipop + compact RIASEC relationship overview; these are the most recognizable current analytical state.
3. **Career direction zone** — top career environments with explainability entry point, not merely percentages.
4. **Work Values / Career Signals** — ranked/structured visuals with less visual mass than the primary profile.
5. **Change over time** — shown only when enough valid historical data exists.
6. **Assessment history / report / privacy/export** — operational rows/table/links, lower visual priority.

The user’s dashboard references demonstrate that a dashboard can contain many bounded surfaces without feeling like “cards everywhere” because the surfaces have different jobs: chart canvas, table, calendar, profile/photo, task list, timeline, map, schedule. Personality must achieve the same semantic variety.

## C19. Data visualization specification — every chart answers a question

| Product question | User question | Preferred representation | Integrity rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Big Five current profile | Where does each continuous dimension currently sit? | Custom lollipop / position plot on shared 0–100 scale | Direct labels + numeric value; no permanent rainbow traits. Missing = “not available”. |
| RIASEC current interests | What is the overall relationship among the six interest dimensions, and which lead? | Radar for gestalt + ranked bars/labels for precision | Radar is not the sole precise reading; direct ranking/text remains visible. |
| Work Values | Which motivations are strongest relative to the rest? | Ranked horizontal bars with direct labels | Top values may receive marker emphasis; do not render twelve giant rounded rows. |
| Career Signals | Which demonstrated capability signals are strongest and what evidence supports them? | Ranked bars or evidence-linked capability map | Only render scores/evidence actually returned. |
| Career fit | Why is this role more/less aligned? | Grouped/bullet bars for real component fits; total fit as supporting index | Do not fabricate component values from total. Avoid donut for arbitrary “fit percentage”. |
| Trait evolution | How has a dimension changed across completed assessments? | Line chart / small multiples on shared scale | Requires enough history. Keep table/summary alternative. |
| Skill gaps | Which skills are present, missing critical, or recommended? | Ranked gap bars or compact matrix | Status text/icons alongside color. |
| Roadmap | What is complete and what is next? | Milestone timeline/progress list | A progress bar is secondary; milestones carry meaning. |
| Assessment history | What happened, when, and can I open it? | Structured list/table | Not a chart. |
| Evidence composition | What evidence sources exist? | Only use part-to-whole chart if backend returns a genuine source distribution | Otherwise use evidence list/field; no decorative donut. |

### Chart styling

Charts should feel like the same product without becoming a branded art poster. Use neutral grid/track colors, direct labels, tabular numerals, restrained semantic accent and adequate hit targets. Grid lines are allowed inside a chart only where they improve quantitative reading; the ban on decorative page-grid lines does not remove functional axes. If an axis can be replaced by direct labels and spacing without reducing comprehension, prefer the cleaner treatment.

Interactive charts expose the important value without requiring hover. Pointer/keyboard/touch inspection adds detail. Every chart has an accessible title/description and a tabular/text alternative for users who cannot perceive the graphic. Recharts can generate standard paths/scales, but custom SVG/DOM remains preferable for very simple lollipop plots because it avoids a generic library skin.

## C20. Results experience — progressive disclosure without a wall of report cards

The result route should feel like opening an inspectable professional profile, not scrolling through a generated report document.

**Opening state.** A concise profile statement and status/provenance information. No “Verified Result” eyebrow. If evidence/confidence metadata is missing, say so rather than implying validity through styling.

**Four readings.** The same visualization language established on the marketing demo becomes real user data: Big Five lollipop, RIASEC overview/ranking, Work Values hierarchy, Career Signals. The user can navigate among them without losing the summary. On mobile, one reading per view.

**Evidence and confidence.** Real evidence sources and missing context. No invented “calibrated and valid” status. Confidence metadata gets a plain explanation and methodology link.

**Career relationships.** The top roles are not three empty giant cards. Use compact ranked roles and one active explainable detail with fit breakdown, stretch/gaps and development actions.

**Roadmap.** Timeline/milestone structure rather than four numbered marketing steps.

**Methodology/boundary.** Short factual statement with link to full methodology/trust, not a massive disclaimer card.

**Utilities.** PDF export, share, retake and career explorer live in a compact tool row. Coach is available at the end or as a persistent launcher, but it must not occlude important result content or imply that AI is the source of the deterministic profile.

## C21. Career Explorer blueprint

Desktop uses a dense master-detail architecture: role index/list at left, active role detail at center/right, one environment photograph when it adds professional context, fit breakdown, skills and roadmap. The master list may be a bounded list because each role is an independent selectable object. It should not look like seven oversized marketing cards.

Mobile uses a true two-stage flow: role list → role detail. “Back to roles” returns without losing scroll/search state. Motion for React or native/View Transition shared-element behavior can carry the selected role title/media into detail. Search/analyze-another-role remains available only where the real backend endpoint supports it.

Career photo selection must become profession-specific. A developer image is not a universal “career” image. UX should show design/research work, research scientist shows empirical work, operations shows actual operational/technical context, and so on. Photography is evidence/world-building, not decorative category art.

## C22. Privacy/account settings blueprint

Privacy is operational UI. It does not need marketing theatre. The page should group **data source**, **export**, **selective deletion**, **account deletion**, and **AI processing boundary** through plain hierarchy, structured rows and confirmation dialogs. Destructive actions require clear consequence copy, explicit confirmation, and accessible focus management.

The route may use one small illustration only if it improves comprehension/empty state; privacy does not need a large hero character. The visual language should communicate control and seriousness without dark “security” theatrics.

The unresolved retention-source divergence is a hard blocker for precise retention-duration copy. Until reconciled, describe controls that the code actually exposes and avoid promises about indefinite retention or automatic purge behavior.

## C23. Illustration system — quality gate and integration

The approved **category** is flat editorial workplace character illustration. The exact assets are not yet approved. The illustrations supplied by the user show a preferred grammar: clean white/transparent background, human proportions that feel contemporary rather than childish, controlled outlines or clean vector edges, restrained navy/teal/warm colors, contextual objects such as tablet, magnifier, clipboard, charts or professional tools, and enough white space that the character can participate in a dashboard composition.

### Phase 3C illustration family requirements

- One drawing family across dashboard, analytics empty states, development and assessment completion.
- Similar head/body proportion and line/edge treatment.
- Similar facial-detail level.
- Similar object perspective and shadow treatment.
- Transparent or composition-compatible background.
- Recolorable palette that fits the final Personality system without forcing every illustration into monochrome.
- Professional activity must match scene truth: analyzing, reviewing, organizing, collaborating, researching, building—not generic celebration.
- Multi-person scenes are encouraged when teamwork/development is the actual subject.
- No random floating stars, rockets, trophy/confetti, AI brains or productivity icon clouds.
- No giant abstract blob behind every person.

### Source research

DrawKit is currently the strongest licensing/coherence candidate because its commercial license permits modification and does not require attribution, and it contains coherent “People Working,” “Teamwork,” “Product & Project Managers” and data/technology families. ManyPixels remains another commercial/no-attribution source. Storyset can provide strong compositions but its free-license flow requires attribution. These platforms are sources, not automatic approvals.

### Rive decision

Rive is an option only if we intentionally author an illustration that benefits from state-machine animation. The React/WebGL runtime introduces meaningful WASM/rendering weight, so static SVG plus DOM/Motion/Anime animation remains the default. If we use Rive, the state machine must communicate an actual product state—completion, progress, analysis—not loop endlessly as decoration.

## C24. Media selection and provenance system

The media registry remains a first-class production artifact. Every approved image must carry: source page, source ID, creator where available, license terms/date checked, local derivative files, focal point, desktop/tablet/mobile crop, alt text, narrative job, motion participation, and whether a model/property/trademark release creates any usage concern.

### Current explicit media status

- **KEEP — Pexels 9618456:** top view architectural project workspace. Primary hero environment.
- **KEEP — Pexels 5940721:** professional/laptop/document context. Supporting hero/human-scale evidence.
- **RESEARCH / REPLACE — Pexels 7988086:** current developer/coding image. It is semantically relevant but visually generic relative to the new threshold.
- **REJECTED — `RLDjPI-r5fU`:** factory/machine imagery.
- **REJECTED — `xRb2zIFY4_E`:** art director checking monitor.
- **SUPPORTING CONSIDERATION:** previously accepted architectural/scientific/creative/coding/model-making references remain research candidates unless a later rejection supersedes them.

A promising code-image replacement research direction is a photograph that combines digital coding with physical sketching/plans rather than showing only code on a monitor. No replacement is approved until the user sees the exact candidate.

### Hero actor-count rule

The opening should not depend on finding five miraculous stock images. Three strong actors beat five acceptable ones. Architecture is the protagonist; the human/professional photo humanizes it; one process fragment can imply broader evidence. Work Worlds and Career Relationships are where the larger profession/image library can expand.

## C25. Mobile-first art direction — independent composition standard

A route fails Phase 3C if the desktop screenshot is exceptional and the phone merely “works.” The acceptance standard is stronger:

> **Someone who only sees the 390px experience should believe that 390px was one of the primary design canvases.**

### Required mobile transformations

- Hero: one dominant portrait crop + one supporting actor, not hidden desktop constellation + generic card rail.
- Work Worlds: touch-native active media with next/previous context; no desktop pin.
- Context theatre: separate understandable states; no long forced pin.
- Profile: one reading owns viewport; precise touch/keyboard equivalent.
- Career: list → detail, not squashed 3-column master-detail.
- Dashboard: next action/profile first; analytical detail progressively disclosed.
- Charts: one chart at a time with horizontal inspection only when the chart genuinely needs width.
- Assessment: question and answers optimized for thumb/reading; no hover; safe-area aware.
- Auth: form first; image secondary.
- Footer: large type re-authored to avoid accidental crop/overflow.

### Mobile overflow policy

No `body { overflow-x: hidden; }` or global container clipping as a repair. At 320/360/375/390/430 px, measure `scrollWidth - clientWidth`; it must be approximately 0–1 px. If not, identify the actor responsible: fixed width, transform, min-width, negative margin, absolute plane, chart, media rail or unbreakable text.

## C26. Accessibility, input modalities and reduced motion

Accessibility is not a closure checklist; it changes the visual architecture.
