- Every public route has correct landmarks and one main target.
- Keyboard users can navigate every gallery state without dragging.
- Scroll-driven scenes also have direct controls where state matters.
- Focus remains visible on light/dark/media backgrounds.
- Header/menu overlays trap and restore focus correctly.
- Touch targets target approximately 44–48px minimum.
- Charts have accessible names, descriptions and data alternatives.
- Color is never the sole indication of score/state/selection.
- Form errors are associated with fields and announced.
- Reduced-motion users receive a shorter static/direct-control composition, not a pinned 4000px journey with animations disabled.
- Autoplay/video/animation that can distract must respect motion preferences and pause rules.
- Image alt text describes the information/scene role rather than repeating decorative captions.
- Any canvas/Rive/WebGL enhancement has semantic DOM content beneath or beside it.

## C27. Performance and rendering architecture

The visual concept is constrained by performance from the beginning. A strong composition that becomes janky on a normal laptop or phone is not premium.

### Progressive media/rendering ladder

**Tier A:** semantic DOM, CSS, responsive static media, accessible forms/charts.  
**Tier B:** DOM + orchestration (GSAP/Motion/Anime) + optimized video/SVG/vector illustration.  
**Tier C:** realtime Canvas/WebGL/Rive renderer only when the visual problem genuinely requires it.

Personality Phase 3C is primarily Tier A+B. The product does not currently need a continuous WebGL world.

### Hero/LCP

The architecture image is LCP-critical. It must be locally served in optimized responsive formats, sized correctly, and visible immediately—never behind an entrance opacity animation. Preload only the source likely to be LCP for the current breakpoint. Avoid preloading every hero fragment.

### Fonts

Preload the minimum Mona Sans variable file(s) actually used above the fold. Verify that a single variable file does not become needlessly large compared with split role files. Use `font-display` behavior that avoids invisible text. Motion should wait for stable layout only where measurements require it; it should not hold the page behind a fake loader.

### Scroll timelines

Keep transforms/compositor-friendly properties where possible. Avoid layout thrash from repeatedly calling `getBoundingClientRect()` inside high-frequency scroll callbacks when a FLIP/GSAP measurement can be cached/invalidated on refresh. Refresh triggers after fonts/media dimensions settle. Do not pin transformed ancestors in ways that create browser inconsistencies.

### Charts/illustration

Lazy-load heavy below-fold chart bundles only where meaningful, but do not create suspenseful loaders for small data views. Static SVG illustrations are cheap; Rive/WebGL runtime is justified only when interactivity adds clear value.

## C28. Code architecture — prevent another template rebuild

The source should become **systematic at the primitive level and authored at the scene level**.

### Reusable low-level primitives are encouraged

- responsive image/picture component;
- focus/button/link primitives;
- accessible dialog/menu;
- chart scale/label helpers;
- media provenance registry;
- motion preference/context;
- smooth-scroll bridge;
- direct scroll-state navigation helper;
- form fields;
- list/table primitives;
- tooltip/popover;
- typography/color/spacing tokens.

### Macro abstractions to avoid

- universal `MarketingSection`;
- universal `FeatureCard`;
- universal `DashboardWidget` as the visual solution for every datum;
- universal `SectionIntro` with eyebrow/title/body;
- universal `AnimatedSection` fade-up wrapper;
- universal `ProductMarketingPage` schema;
- universal `PremiumCard` / `BentoItem`;
- a single `col-span-*` grid deciding dashboard hierarchy at all breakpoints.

Page/scene components are allowed to be deliberately specific: `EvidenceHeroScene`, `WorkWorldsTheatre`, `ContextSignalTheatre`, `ProfileLensStage`, `CareerRelationshipStage`, etc. Specific names make visual intention harder for a coding agent to erase.

### Source cleanup targets

- Add/fix `publicMedia.auth` or decouple auth media from the marketing registry so Login/Signup cannot crash.
- Replace global ScrollSmoother only after Lenis integration passes forward/reverse/anchor/focus tests.
- Remove unused `@barba/core` if no route lifecycle depends on it.
- Keep Framer Motion dependency if used for authenticated layout transitions; otherwise migrate intentionally to modern Motion package/API rather than mixing versions casually.
- Remove obsolete legacy Bootstrap styles from redesigned routes or isolate them so they cannot leak.
- Replace universal `.dashboard-widget` composition with semantic primitives, while retaining a surface primitive for genuine independent objects.
- Preserve accessibility tables as alternatives when visual charts are added.

## C29. Phase 3C implementation sequence — design gates before Codex

The next coding pass should not begin until the remaining visual-resource gates are explicitly approved. The correct order is:

1. **Approve/adjust Phase 3C palette.** Review on hero, dashboard and 390px assessment frame simultaneously.
2. **Approve hero actor set.** Architecture + laptop/professional already retained; approve/reject one optional process/code fragment replacement.
3. **Approve Work Worlds media set.** Existing images may survive selectively, but each world gets crop and aspect review.
4. **Approve one illustration family and exact scenes.** Do not let Codex download random “matching” illustrations.
5. **Lock Phase 3C typography role/axis tokens.** Mona Sans 2 remains current candidate.
6. **Lock public scene geometry and motion timelines.** This supplement is the architectural baseline; rendered static frames can refine coordinates.
7. **Lock authenticated dashboard/data visualization hierarchy.** Ensure endpoint/data truth supports each plot.
8. **Write one exhaustive Codex implementation contract.** It may be long; ambiguity is more expensive than prompt length.
9. **Codex implements source only.** It reports blockers rather than inventing design.
10. **Render/browser review.** Desktop and mobile screenshots + recordings are compared against the visual contract.
11. **Correct composition, not symptoms.** If the result is wrong, redesign the scene rather than adding another wrapper/card/padding patch.
12. **Consolidated tests/build/performance/accessibility validation** after the meaningful visual block closes.

The user retains final approval over visual resources. A resource marked PENDING or SHORTLISTED does not enter the implementation brief as though approved.

## C30. Phase 3C rendered-browser acceptance matrix

| Viewport | Required visual check |
| --- | --- |
| 1440×900 | Hero hierarchy/crop; complete Work Worlds state; pinned theatre centered; dashboard density; no accidental giant whitespace. |
| 1366×768 | Height-critical check. Every pinned state must keep media + semantic text + controls inside viewport. |
| 1024×768 | Tablet/desktop boundary; no awkward hybrid of pin and touch layout. |
| 768×1024 | Tablet portrait composition; charts/rails recompose; header/menu behavior. |
| 430×932 | Large phone; safe areas; media crops; bottom nav; no desktop stack fingerprint. |
| 390×844 | Primary mobile acceptance canvas. One actor/task per state; no page overflow. |
| 375×812 | Common narrow phone; line breaks and hit targets. |
| 360×800 | Narrow phone stress test. |
| 320×568 | Minimum-width/short-height stress; auth/assessment/dashboard remain usable. |

### Motion QA for every flagship timeline

- slow forward scroll;
- fast forward wheel/trackpad burst;
- slow reverse;
- fast reverse;
- oscillation across every state boundary;
- direct Previous/Next state jump;
- keyboard navigation;
- resize while before/inside/after the pin;
- browser back/forward restoration;
- reduced motion;
- touch/pointer switch on hybrid devices where possible;
- focus movement while a scene is pinned;
- asset late-load refresh.

A transition is not accepted until its most important state can be held visibly at a stable scroll position. If a screenshot taken at the intended dwell point misses the actor or explanatory copy, the timeline is wrong even if the animation looks good at normal speed.

## C31. Codex handoff contract — Phase 3C exactness standard

Every implementation prompt must include the following sections. Omitting one means Codex has permission to invent it, which is precisely what the design-authority model is meant to prevent.

### 1. Product/experience thesis
What the page means, what the user must understand, and what emotional/operational state it should create.

### 2. Route and state inventory
Every route involved, loading/empty/error/data states, authentication state, and any URL/query/session behavior that must not break.

### 3. Exact copy
Final/working strings clearly distinguished. Unsupported claims prohibited. No implementation-agent copywriting.

### 4. Media registry
Exact local files, source/provenance, desktop/tablet/mobile crop, focal point, sizes, alt, loading priority, fallback, motion participation.

### 5. Typography tokens
Family/file/license, axes, exact size/line-height/weight/width/optical roles, line breaks, tabular numerals, mobile variants.

### 6. Color tokens
Exact values, semantic roles, state meaning, chart palette, dark-scene behavior, forbidden combinations.

### 7. Composition states
Actor count, coordinates/alignment logic, width AND height envelopes, z-order, negative space, overlap, clipping, containment, scene pressure.

### 8. Motion state machine
Pin boundaries, timeline labels/progress ranges, dwell, direct controls, forward/reverse, reduced motion, touch equivalent.

### 9. Responsive recomposition
Desktop/tablet/mobile are described separately. “Stack” is not a sufficient instruction.

### 10. Accessibility
Focus, keyboard, touch, announcements, chart alternatives, reduced motion, contrast, forms/errors, landmarks.

### 11. Performance
LCP image, preload policy, lazy media, animation properties, bundle additions, renderer fallback.

### 12. Failure fallback
What happens if media fails, animation library fails, smoothing fails, chart data is missing, AI report is unavailable, WebGL is unavailable.

### 13. Explicit negative controls
Paste the relevant rejection list into the brief. Codex must be told what not to recreate.

### 14. Acceptance checklist
Rendered screenshots/recordings at required viewports; no overflow; source tests; build; motion QA; truthful data; no banned patterns.

### Codex authority boundary
Codex may decide implementation mechanics that preserve the specified experience. It may not change copy, assets, colors, fonts, macro composition, mobile architecture, chart type, motion semantics, or product claims. If an instruction cannot be implemented as specified, Codex reports the blocker and proposes **mechanism** alternatives without redesigning the experience.

## C35. User-provided 20-reference composition board — numbered interpretation ledger

| Reference | Observed composition intelligence | Transfer rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 — Aloha surf interface | Large environment plus vertically stacked profile/media navigation. | Learn active-media hierarchy and quiet central field; do not copy surf typography/palette. |
| 2 — Saint Antonin travel/gallery | Full-bleed environment with one active vertical card and neighboring travel panels. | Active image + neighbors + background world; useful for Work Worlds semantics. |
| 3 — long commerce page | Different media scales and modules across one long page. | Scene grammar changes; image participates in section identity; do not transfer ecommerce patterns. |
| 4 — travel stories desktop/mobile | Dense editorial media hierarchy with explicit mobile art direction. | Mobile is separately composed, not merely smaller desktop. |
| 5 — Spaciaz real estate | Large image/typography, dark proof field, mixed scale. | Pressure variation and high visual contrast; not the lime/real-estate surface. |
| 6 — Japan tour | Environment-led hero and timeline-like travel explanation. | Persistent place + sequence; not travel aesthetics. |
| 7 — yacht travel | Photography carries color while UI stays restrained. | Media-led color system and clear transaction chapters. |
| 8 — photographer editorial | Irregular image sizes and editorial collage. | Purposeful asymmetry and object-specific shapes. |
| 9 — Flowblox | Portraits form an arc around a centered proposition. | Repetition can create spatial form rather than grid. |
| 10 — LeonHome | Hero image and giant type interlock with expert/media chapters. | Image/type spatial relation; not luxury-real-estate identity. |
| 11 — Atacama | Text, landscape and rounded image/action composition with strong scale changes. | Media can participate inside a sentence/visual grammar; no universal rounded shapes. |
| 12 — expedition outdoor | Mixed image panels, list/table-like date info and large environmental images. | Use correct representation for dates/process/media rather than cards everywhere. |
| 13 — blue AI SaaS | Cloud/tech art direction and UI cards. | Useful as negative saturation warning; do not import blue/green AI-tech reflex. |
| 14 — forest tours | Persistent natural background and image clusters. | World persistence; avoid decorative hexagon gimmick unless product-native. |
| 15 — Machu Picchu | Atmospheric dark environment with diamond images and timeline. | Image geometry can be authored, but shape must come from concept. |
| 16 — Scandi tours | Dark, media-heavy mobile/desktop layouts with layered images. | Strong media rhythm and route-specific composition. |
| 17 — dark travel narrative | Full-background chapters, overlapping media, reviews. | Pressure/release and environmental continuity; not glass/dark template surface. |
| 18 — Orizon real estate | One large UI/product object becomes protagonist. | Complex page can revolve around one designed object rather than components. |
| 19 — TrendZone fashion | Unequal-height portraits around large type. | Unequal scale and type/media interlock create authored hierarchy. |
| 20 — Art Circles | Radial/orbit collection around active curator/artwork. | Collections can be spatially related rather than linear grid; only use orbit when semantics justify. |


## C36. Complete “never regress” source-review checklist

- No new `eyebrow`, `kicker`, `pretitle`, `stage-tag`, `world-01` style labels on public routes.
- No generic `dashboard-widget` wrapper around information that is better as chart/list/table/open field.
- No `MetricCard` row as the first solution to a dashboard section.
- No page-level `overflow-x: hidden/clip` as a fix for an unknown overflow bug.
- No hero actor count increase without an explicit hierarchy rationale.
- No image plane sized only in `vw` inside a pinned height-critical stage.
- No `aspect-ratio` used without checking resulting block height at 1366×768.
- No direct `display:none` swap inside a flagship scroll sequence where continuity is part of the concept.
- No IntersectionObserver threshold described as “smooth transformation” without persistent actors.
- No direct scroll state update that can happen before the stage is established in the central viewport.
- No fade-up reveal wrapper applied to every section.
- No bounce/spring default on marketing controls.
- No scroll smoothing on touch by default.
- No two smooth-scroll engines at once.
- No Anime + GSAP both controlling the same transform/progress timeline.
- No Barba navigation lifecycle layered over React Router without a proven requirement.
- No WebGL/R3F scene because dependencies already exist.
- No Rive runtime just to make a stock illustration wiggle.
- No body copy wider than its reading role supports.
- No arbitrary hero line wrapping; line breaks reviewed at required viewports.
- No selected-state color that also means success/high score elsewhere.
- No trait/category rainbow as permanent identity.
- No `0%` for missing score.
- No dashboard chart without a user question.
- No donut/pie unless a genuine part-to-whole relationship exists.
- No radar as the only precise RIASEC readout.
- No chart without accessible summary/data alternative.
- No career image reused across unrelated professions solely for convenience.
- No generic illustration family mixing.
- No auth route depending on a missing media registry key.
- No mobile “all spans become 12” as the full responsive strategy.
- No desktop pinned motion copied to phone.
- No footer decorative vertical/horizontal grid lines.
- No unsupported privacy/science/training/certification claim.
- No fake loader or artificial minimum wait.
- No implementation agent choosing final palette, font, photograph, illustration, copy, chart type or mobile macro layout.


## C37. Phase 3C decision summary for future products

The most valuable lesson from Personality Assessor is not a particular hero, palette, animation library or dashboard chart. It is a process correction that should reduce failure across KT Courier, Dubai Booking Marketplace, Math Intellect, Aegis Senital and the Valtum flagship:

1. **Do not confuse design-system consistency with macro-composition repetition.** Share primitives; author scenes.
2. **Do not evaluate implementation reports as visual acceptance.** The browser is the authority.
3. **Do not patch rejected composition with more CSS.** Diagnose the representation model and redesign it.
4. **Do not let cards become the default answer to layout uncertainty.** Choose representation from information behavior.
5. **Do not size media from width alone.** Height, focal point, crop, text and state controls are one visual equation.
6. **Do not call a threshold swap a flow.** Flow requires continuity, visibility, dwell and reverse coherence.
7. **Do not make mobile a breakpoint afterthought.** Compose it independently.
8. **Do not choose one animation library as an ideology.** Give each tool a clearly owned interaction domain.
9. **Do not add technology to compensate for weak art direction.** Strong DOM/CSS/image/SVG composition can outperform a purposeless realtime renderer.
10. **Do not allow a rejected pattern to disappear from memory.** The rejection ledger is part of the Master Brain and must be consulted before every future brief.

Phase 3C therefore represents a maturation of the Brain: from a collection of strong design principles into an enforceable **research → art-direction → engineering → browser-validation operating system**.

## C38. Component-level Phase 3C implementation specification

### PublicHeader

Integrated with opening world; transparent/light field initially; tone change follows scene contrast, not arbitrary scroll distance. Desktop nav uses plain links with strong focus. CTA uses ink/dark by default. Mobile menu is a full functional panel, not pill chrome. Header height included in pinned-scene geometry.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

### ResponsiveImage

Preserve current picture/srcset infrastructure. Add explicit `mobileMedia`/crop metadata if needed instead of filename conventions alone. Do not assume `object-fit:cover` solves art direction. Surface focal point and intrinsic dimensions from registry.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

### EvidenceHeroScene

Reduce to 2–3 media actors. Use CSS custom properties or scene tokens for actor envelopes; compute collision-safe layout at height breakpoints. Remove hero-plane labels. LCP dominant media eager/high priority; others lazy unless visible immediately.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

### WorkWorldsTheatre

Stable pin shell; inner rail transforms. Each world data object includes `desktopCrop`, `mobileCrop`, `copy`, `evidenceRole`. ScrollTrigger labels for six established states. Direct navigation scrolls to label. Active image/copy remain inside viewport.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

### ContextSignalTheatre

Persistent DOM groups for context artifact, annotations, question, options and signal. Timeline animates opacity/position/clip/scale while preserving semantics. User manual selection suspends/demo-overrides auto selection cleanly.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

### ProfileLensStage

One central data region. Direct lens controls are semantic buttons. Scroll and button state share one controller. Visuals rendered from demo data publicly and real data authenticated. Avoid unmounting entire stage per lens.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

### EvidenceField

Evidence items use list/artifact markup; relationships can use SVG lines if needed. Missing context is expressed textually and spatially, not via dashed “empty card”.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

### CareerRelationshipStage

Role master list may be a structured selectable list. Active detail uses one profession media source, open copy and chart. Motion uses Motion layout/shared elements rather than scroll pin by default.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

### DevelopmentStage

Approved SVG illustration plus DOM/SVG evidence token. Steps are not cards. Active state indicated by route/marker/typography. Reduced motion shows all steps statically.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

### TerminalFooter

Final scene responsive type tokens; utility nav; no decorative rule grid. Verify 320px no overflow.

**Implementation review questions:** Does this component still make sense if border radius is removed? Is the boundary semantic? Does it fit at 1366×768 and 390×844? Does missing data remain truthful? Can keyboard/touch operate it? Does reduced motion preserve the same information? Does the component introduce any rejected global visual grammar?

## C39. Final Phase 3C statement

Phase 3C does not declare the frontend finished. It declares the **design and engineering problem correctly framed**. The next implementation is allowed only after the remaining resource gates are approved. The target is not “more creative than Awwwards.” The target is a Personality Assessor experience whose composition, motion, data visualization, media, mobile behavior and application states feel inevitable because they come from the actual product.

The design authority must continue to research before each significant decision. That requirement does not mean endlessly postponing implementation; it means research is attached to a decision. Once a decision is sufficiently evidenced, it is locked, handed to Codex exactly, rendered, tested and either accepted or rejected from the browser. That is how the project moves quickly without repeating the same expensive mistakes.

**Final current doctrine:**

> Product truth first.  
> World, protagonist and evidence before component choice.  
> Representation before container.  
> Geometry before decoration.  
> Media crop before `object-fit`.  
> State visibility before motion spectacle.  
> Dwell before release.  
> Mobile as a primary composition.  
> Charts answer questions.  
> Illustrations belong to one authored family.  
> Color remains semantic and restrained.  
> Typography is an operating system.  
> One motion owner per interaction domain.  
> Progressive enhancement beneath every ambitious scene.  
> Codex implements; it does not art-direct.  
> The browser decides whether the design is actually good.  
> Rejections remain part of the Brain so they do not return under new class names.


---

# PHASE 3C EXTENDED FORENSIC APPENDICES

## C40. Rejection postmortems — what failed, why it failed, and the prevention mechanism

### Card abundance

**Failure anatomy.** The visible symptom was an endless run of bordered rounded white surfaces. The deeper cause was that implementation began from a component library rather than information behavior. Once `dashboard-widget`, content cards, framework layers and option cards existed, almost every new information group was naturally inserted into one of them. The design system made the wrong thing easy.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Do not merely lower card radius or remove shadows. Rebuild route compositions from semantic representations. Keep a bounded surface only when it represents an independent object or interactive region. During source review, count macro surfaces and ask what would happen if their border/background vanished. If the meaning survives unchanged, the boundary was probably decorative.

### Stretched horizontal cards

**Failure anatomy.** After the user rejected small/equal cards, some implementations stretched information modules across the width. This looked different in CSS but preserved the same mental model: heading + paragraph inside a large rectangle. The page still read like a component catalog and gained excessive vertical height.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Treat long-form explanation as typography in open space, a structured list, a diagram, or an artifact. A full-width bounded region is allowed for a real form/table/upload/interactive object, not because the designer needs a section background.

### Hero overcrowding

**Failure anatomy.** The architecture image, evidence wall, developer, scientist and student fragments were each individually defensible. Together they created five competing focal planes. Typography then had to fight those planes, so line breaks and overlaps became accidental.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Set an actor budget before CSS. Phase 3C hero maximum is three meaningful media actors, with one clearly dominant. Draw a negative-space map and collision envelope before setting percentages. Every supporting actor must answer a different narrative job.

### Good image, bad placement

**Failure anatomy.** The user’s strongest hero feedback proved that media quality cannot compensate for geometry. The architecture image is liked, yet the hero still looks weak because type crosses detailed areas, supporting images have comparable pressure, and the stage wastes/contests space.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Approve images and composition separately. Each media asset gets a narrative role, crop, focal point, width range, height range, z-order, neighboring actor and allowable text-overlap zone. A liked image remains liked even if the current placement is rejected.

### Width-only media sizing

**Failure anatomy.** The Work Worlds panel was driven mainly by `vw` and a 16:10 ratio. At shorter desktop heights, the image grew beyond the available stage block size. The user then could not see its copy while the page was pinned.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Every cinematic/pinned media actor has both inline and block constraints. Validate at 1366×768, not only 1440×900. If media + copy + header cannot be visible together, the scene has failed viewport ownership.

### Transitions outside the useful viewport

**Failure anatomy.** Intersection thresholds or progress callbacks changed content after the actor had already moved too far above/below the focal region. Technically the transition fired; perceptually the visitor missed it.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Design the viewport first: establish the actor, then transform, then dwell. State boundaries are chosen from what the person sees, not arbitrary percentages. Record scroll video and freeze frames at every label.

### Hard state swapping

**Failure anatomy.** React state replaced one panel/image with another. Even when opacity transitions were added, the visitor perceived a hard content swap rather than one idea transforming.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Flagship scenes retain persistent actors. Where a concept changes form, animate geometry/opacity/crop/position of persistent DOM/SVG elements or use layout/shared-element transitions. React conditional mounting remains fine for ordinary UI where transformation is not part of meaning.

### Motion without dwell

**Failure anatomy.** Early attempts consumed the entire scroll interval with continuous change, leaving no stable state long enough to understand. This can look “smooth” in a screen recording but feels slippery during real use.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Allocate progress plateaus after meaningful state arrivals. Dwell is part of the timeline contract. Direct navigation should land on dwell labels, not midway through transitions.

### Global smoothness as a design substitute

**Failure anatomy.** ScrollSmoother made scroll interpolation softer but did not make scene boundaries, actor geometry or state swaps coherent. Smooth input cannot repair poor state choreography.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Separate scroll interpolation from motion design. Native scroll remains the semantic source; Lenis/ScrollSmoother only changes feel. Scene timelines own visible transformations. Disable smoothing without losing the story.

### Citron as identity shortcut

**Failure anatomy.** The bright signal color improved contrast but grew into large buttons/active surfaces. It began to resemble a contemporary AI/SaaS visual fingerprint and competed with the photography.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Use accent sparingly and semantically. Test an ink CTA plus photography-led color. If a warm marker is approved, limit it to small state/data events. The brand should remain recognizable in grayscale.

### Dark scenes with poor foreground hierarchy

**Failure anatomy.** Earlier brown/chocolate and dark-on-dark combinations made sections unreadable and shifted the category toward food/lifestyle. A dark field can be excellent, but only if the product/world earns it and contrast is controlled.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Use dark fields selectively for theatre/media chapters. Define explicit dark foreground/muted tokens. Check every live header/navigation state. Never use dark merely to signal “premium.”

### Decorative micro-labels

**Failure anatomy.** Words such as `World 01`, stage tags, tiny all-caps framework labels and floating hero captions recreated the exact eyebrow/pill grammar the user had banned.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** A label must either be required for navigation/data meaning or disappear. Major section meaning is expressed through the heading/composition. Avoid tiny uppercase decorative hierarchy.

### Artificial selected-state styling

**Failure anatomy.** Default first item was shown with tinted fill/colored border because a component needed a selected state. The user recognized this as template/AI design.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Selection state should emerge from task semantics: position, type weight, outline, check/marker, media dominance, or actual content change. First item may be selected because the system has a default, but the visual should not become a fashion motif.

### Universal dashboard widget

**Failure anatomy.** A reusable widget made implementation consistent but flattened all dashboard information into the same white surface. Charts, timelines, history, status, profile and actions lost differentiated semantics.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Keep low-level surface primitives but compose dashboard zones by data behavior. Chart canvas, table, list, illustration/empty state, timeline and selectable object should visibly differ.

### Metric-card KPI rows

**Failure anatomy.** Six small summary cards felt like a standard SaaS analytics template. Some values also did not deserve equal emphasis.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Create an attention hierarchy: one dominant current profile/next action, secondary chart relationships, compact statuses, and lower-priority utilities. Only truly independent top metrics get tile treatment.

### Table-only “chart”

**Failure anatomy.** The source correctly preserved accessible numeric data, but the user expected an actual analytical visualization. Naming a table `TraitTrendChart` did not meet the visual/product task.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Add a real line/small-multiple plot while keeping the table/summary. Visual and accessible representations complement rather than replace each other.

### Desktop stack as mobile

**Failure anatomy.** The 12-column grid collapsed every span to 12 columns. This is a standard responsive implementation but did not create a mobile app experience.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Explicit mobile route architecture: reorder priority, remove simultaneous panes, use list→detail, one chart/lens per view, touch-native galleries, safe-area navigation and art-directed media crops.

### Generic mobile media rail

**Failure anatomy.** Hiding the desktop hero and showing four 78vw portrait cards technically prevented overlap but converted the opening into a generic carousel.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Recompose hero for phone: one dominant image, one supporting fragment, phone-specific line break and CTA order. Use a rail only when the collection itself is the product meaning.

### Illustration as a pasted card decoration

**Failure anatomy.** An illustration was placed inside a large bounded panel next to long step cards. The art became decoration rather than a participant in the composition.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Choose one coherent family and design around the illustration’s silhouette/negative space. Let process labels/evidence tokens relate spatially to the figure. Static SVG is sufficient unless authored interaction requires more.

### Overflow clipping

**Failure anatomy.** `overflow-x: clip` made the page stop showing some overflow but did not establish which actor caused it. This can also clip intentional focus rings, transformed actors or route-transition layers.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Measure and fix the responsible element at every mobile width. Overflow acceptance is numeric (`scrollWidth-clientWidth`). Global clipping requires a scene-specific reason, not as a safety net.

### Implementation-report acceptance

**Failure anatomy.** Several reports claimed source compliance and clean tests while the rendered experience remained visibly wrong. Source-level checklists rewarded local changes without judging composition.

**Phase 3C prevention mechanism.** Every source completion is provisional until rendered-browser art-direction review. Capture defined viewports and recordings. The acceptance artifact is visual behavior plus test/build integrity, not a prose report.

## C41. Flagship motion state machines — exact conceptual timelines

The following timelines are not final millisecond values; they are the semantic state contracts that implementation must map to actual scroll distance and viewport geometry. Each state has an establish/transform/dwell intent.

### Hero → Work Worlds carry

| State | Name | Visible contract |
| --- | --- | --- |
| H0 | Opening established | Architecture media and headline are fully readable. Supporting actor settled. |
| H1 | Prose quiets | Supporting paragraph/actions reduce pressure only after user has read first viewport. |
| H2 | Evidence cue separates | One small fragment or crop cue moves toward Work Worlds entrance; main architecture remains recognizable. |
| H3 | Dark world arrives | Work Worlds background/field begins entering before carry actor completes, providing directional priming. |
| H4 | Build state established | Carry resolves into or aligns with first Work World without duplicated proxy; hero actor no longer visible as a separate fixed clone. |

**Reverse contract.** Every transition above must reverse without content remount flicker, proxy duplication, lost selection, or a mismatch between direct-control state and scroll state. **Reduced-motion contract.** Replace the long timeline with direct static chapters/controls while preserving state order and copy.

### Work Worlds

| State | Name | Visible contract |
| --- | --- | --- |
| W0 | Build established | Build image + name + copy all visible; previous control disabled but not visually dominant. |
| W1 | Build→Investigate transform | Rail advances; Build becomes prior context, Investigate becomes active. |
| W2 | Investigate dwell | Scientist/research image and text stable. |
| W3 | Investigate→Make | Direction and neighbor cues remain coherent. |
| W4 | Make dwell | Craft detail stable. |
| W5 | Make→Shape | UX/design image enters; no abrupt opacity swap. |
| W6 | Shape dwell | Design context stable. |
| W7 | Shape→Structure | Blueprint/system image assumes active position. |
| W8 | Structure dwell | Structure copy fully visible. |
| W9 | Structure→Collaborate | Collaboration environment enters. |
| W10 | Collaborate dwell | Final world stable; visitor can stop and read. |
| W11 | Resolve/release | Rail and background prepare vertical return; next bridge content enters before pin ends. |

**Reverse contract.** Every transition above must reverse without content remount flicker, proxy duplication, lost selection, or a mismatch between direct-control state and scroll state. **Reduced-motion contract.** Replace the long timeline with direct static chapters/controls while preserving state order and copy.

### Context → Question → Signal

| State | Name | Visible contract |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C0 | Context artifact established | CV/manual baseline visible as one artifact, no annotation competition. |
| C1 | Evidence parsed | Project/experience/skill/education/interest annotations appear around the artifact. |
| C2 | Relevant evidence isolates | One evidence item gains focus; others quiet spatially but remain context. |
| C3 | Question emerges | Focused evidence shifts/anchors into question prompt. Artifact recedes but remains traceable. |
| C4 | Responses established | All answer options readable; no auto-selection yet. |
| C5 | Response exchange/demo | Demo can cycle a response only if marked illustrative and user has not interacted. |
| C6 | Selected response established | One response is clearly selected and readable. |
| C7 | Compress to observed signal | Selected response becomes a compact signal/evidence marker, not a deterministic personality label. |
| C8 | Signal dwell | Signal text remains stable. |
| C9 | Carry to profile | Marker moves/alignment carries into Living Profile stage. |

**Reverse contract.** Every transition above must reverse without content remount flicker, proxy duplication, lost selection, or a mismatch between direct-control state and scroll state. **Reduced-motion contract.** Replace the long timeline with direct static chapters/controls while preserving state order and copy.

### Living Profile

| State | Name | Visible contract |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P0 | Profile intake | Incoming evidence marker lands in central profile field. |
| P1 | Personality established | Big Five lollipop positions and interpretations readable. |
| P2 | Personality→Interests | Tracks reorganize into six-dimension interest relationship. |
| P3 | Interests dwell | Radar/relationship and ranked labels stable. |
| P4 | Interests→Work Values | Dimensions reorder/condense into ranked hierarchy. |
| P5 | Work Values dwell | Top values and lower values readable. |
| P6 | Work Values→Career Signals | Hierarchy transforms to capability/evidence signals. |
| P7 | Career Signals dwell | Capability field readable. |
| P8 | Resolve | Profile is summarized as four independent readings; next Evidence/Confidence chapter begins. |

**Reverse contract.** Every transition above must reverse without content remount flicker, proxy duplication, lost selection, or a mismatch between direct-control state and scroll state. **Reduced-motion contract.** Replace the long timeline with direct static chapters/controls while preserving state order and copy.

## C42. Scene-by-scene responsive geometry checklist

| Scene | Responsive composition rule | Failure test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Hero | At 1440×900, headline and architecture image coexist without accidental overlap; at 1366×768 the architecture image height shrinks before the headline measure collapses; at 1024 decide explicitly whether desktop spatial stage remains or tablet composition takes over; at 390/375/360/320 the architecture crop is portrait/art-directed and only one support image remains. | No image may force horizontal page scroll; supporting image cannot cover CTA/body; header contrast remains valid. |
| Work Worlds | Desktop stage height is governed by `svh`; active media height capped so title/copy/nav remain visible. Tablet likely uses touch rail rather than pin. Mobile active panel is wide enough for visual impact but leaves a next peek. | No min-width 520px leak on phone; no text below a pinned image; no hidden scrollbar required for keyboard control. |
| Context theatre | Desktop pinned artifact has max-width and max-height envelopes. Tablet may keep simplified staged progression if height allows. Phone uses sequential states. | Question/options never overflow viewport width; evidence labels wrap naturally; no absolute annotation offscreen. |
| Profile theatre | Desktop chart region uses container query to adapt label layout. Tablet can use direct tabs. Phone one lens per state with chart labels below/around. | Radar or bars cannot force horizontal scroll; font size remains readable; controls 44px+. |
| Career relationships | Desktop master list + media/detail; tablet 35/65 or list drawer depending width; phone list→detail. | Role names and fit values do not compress into unreadable columns; image uses mobile crop. |
| Development | Desktop illustration shares stage with process path; tablet reduces path complexity; phone stacks illustration and current step with vertical sequence. | No illustration raster scaling blur; no process labels placed as tiny floating text. |
| Dashboard | Desktop asymmetric 12-column may still exist as a low-level layout but not as universal widget template; tablet changes priority; phone shows current state/next action first. | No “span 12 everything” as the only design decision; charts recompose and tables scroll internally only when semantically necessary. |
| Assessment | Desktop question stage max reading width; choices fit first screen where feasible. Phone header compact; question/action safe-area; choices full-width rows. | No fixed height causing keyboard viewport clipping; no horizontal Likert row that becomes 5 tiny unreadable cells. |
| Auth | Desktop form and editorial image have unequal but balanced pressure; tablet form remains dominant; phone media can reduce or move below. | Form always visible even if media fails; no blank route; browser autofill/focus states remain readable. |
| Footer | Desktop terminal type may crop intentionally within safe field; phone gets smaller authored break. | No accidental horizontal overflow; utility links remain accessible. |

## C43. Research-before-each-step protocol — practical operating checklist

This checklist operationalizes the user’s requirement that every future step begins with research rather than relying on a previous conclusion. It is intentionally repetitive at the process level because forgetting the sequence is one of the ways template defaults returned during Phase 3B.

### C43.1 — Define the design question

State the exact decision: hero actor placement, Work Worlds crop, dashboard radar, mobile result layout, illustration source, etc. Do not research “premium websites” broadly when the decision is specific.

### C43.2 — Re-open Master Brain

Search the current Brain for the relevant doctrine, earlier evidence and negative controls. Identify any old candidate decision that has been superseded.

### C43.3 — Search rejection ledger

Find every prior rejection that could recur in this decision. Translate each rejection into a source/visual acceptance test.

### C43.4 — Audit current product/code truth

Read the actual component/data hook/model. Determine available data, states, assets, route constraints and current bugs. Do not design fields the product cannot populate.

### C43.5 — Review user visual references

Open the relevant local reference subset at full size. Analyze composition, scale, crop, hierarchy, mobile behavior, image/type relation, not just style.

### C43.6 — Review external design intelligence

Use Behance/Dribbble/Awwwards for visual vocabulary, and official studio case studies/BTS when behavior/technology matters. Separate directly observed behavior from inference.

### C43.7 — Review platform/technical primary sources

For animation, charts, accessibility, media, responsive layout and performance, read current official docs. Verify compatibility/limitations before prescribing a library.

### C43.8 — Generate multiple product-native hypotheses

At least two viable directions when a resource/identity decision remains open. Explain why each follows from Personality rather than a reference.

### C43.9 — Eliminate anti-pattern directions

Run the anti-copy and anti-template tests. If an unrelated SaaS could use the design unchanged, reject it before implementation.

### C43.10 — Specify desktop and mobile together

Do not approve a desktop composition without simultaneously stating what the phone composition becomes.

### C43.11 — Specify reduced motion and fallback

If the idea depends on a transformation, define its static/direct state before coding.

### C43.12 — Resolve assets/copy/data provenance

Exact image/font/license/copy/metric source is known or explicitly marked pending. Pending does not silently become final.

### C43.13 — Write implementation contract

Only now give Codex exact actor geometry, tokens, timeline states, controls and acceptance criteria.

### C43.14 — Render and compare

Browser screenshots/recordings are compared to the design contract and references for quality, not copied surfaces.

### C43.15 — Record new learning

Every rejection/approval after implementation is appended to the ledger before the next phase.

## C45. Final pre-Codex visual-resource approval board requirements

Before the exhaustive implementation prompt is issued, produce one compact approval board/document containing the following concrete choices. This board is a gate, not an optional moodboard.

| Gate | Approval artifact |
| --- | --- |
| Palette | Render the same candidate tokens on: hero architecture scene, populated dashboard charts, 390px assessment choice state. User approves exact HEX roles. |
| Typography | Render Mona Sans 2 actual strings: “Your work leaves evidence.”, Work Worlds names, one long adaptive question, dashboard labels/numerals, mobile navigation. Approve width/weight/optical ranges. |
| Hero media | Show retained architecture + retained laptop/human + 2–4 candidates for optional third process/code fragment. Approve exact crop/role. |
| Work Worlds | Show all six chosen photographs in their proposed active aspect/crop plus mobile crop. Reject any that feel generic/low-quality. |
| Career media | Profession-specific set for major public/demo careers. Avoid reusing developer image for every technical role. |
| Illustrations | Show 6–10 scenes from one coherent family (or custom set) including welcome, analytics empty, development/team, completion, report/privacy. Approve style first, then exact assets. |
| Charts | Show static visual mock/layout of Big Five lollipop, RIASEC radar+ranking, Work Values bars, trend line and career breakdown in the final palette. |
| Hero/Work Worlds mobile | Show 390px static frames before code: hero first viewport, first Work World active state, profile lens state, dashboard zero state. |

## C46. Closing principle — completeness without paralysis

The requirement to research at every step is not permission to browse forever. Research is attached to a decision and ends when the uncertainty relevant to that decision is sufficiently resolved. The project moves through explicit gates: understand → compare → decide → specify → implement → render → accept/reject → record learning. What Phase 3C forbids is skipping the first four steps and asking a coding agent to discover the design in CSS.

The best evidence that this Brain is working will not be the length of this file. It will be that the next implementation does **not** recreate any of the rejected visual fingerprints under a different class name, that its motion is visible and understandable at the center of the viewport, that mobile feels independently designed, that dashboard charts answer real questions, that images are sized/cropped as art direction, and that the resulting website could not plausibly be mistaken for an unrelated AI-generated SaaS template.


# C47. Phase 3C research-source interpretation archive — what each source changes in our decisions

## C47 — Lusion Oryzo BTS Part 1 — concept/creative direction

The important contribution is continuity. Lusion describes learning that a beautiful isolated section does not create a premium experience if the sections do not belong to the same world. They centered one protagonist and worked hard on transitions between substantially different scenes. For Personality Assessor this validates the evidence-as-protagonist model: architecture/professional evidence does not need to remain literally the same photograph everywhere, but the idea of evidence should survive its transformation into context, question, signal, profile and career relationship. The direct implementation consequence is that our flagship sequences require shared actors and scene handoffs rather than independent “finished sections.” It also reinforces that responsiveness is part of transition design; a scene transition that only works at 1440×900 is not a successful concept.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Lusion Oryzo BTS Part 2 — production pipeline

The key principle is that the pipeline follows the idea. Oryzo used heavy 3D/rendering/scanning experiments because its visual thesis required a physical-feeling product world. Personality does not inherit those technologies. Instead, we inherit the selection logic: first define what must be seen, then choose the cheapest medium that preserves it. A CV artifact and profile chart are better DOM/SVG than WebGL. A professional photograph is better served as responsive AVIF/WebP than a 3D reconstruction. A scroll carry is better owned by GSAP/DOM transforms than by a realtime renderer. This is how we can use fewer technologies while reaching a higher level of craft.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Lusion Oryzo BTS Part 3 — UX/UI/illustration

This source matters because Lusion explicitly rejected an early clean, abstract “Awwwards-like” direction as lifeless. The believable desk/world and protagonist made the site feel specific. They also describe “design without ego”: when imagery/world is rich, typography/UI can become restrained. This maps directly to our hero and color correction. We should not add five labels, a bright CTA, tinted badges and multiple border treatments around already-rich architecture photography. It also supports our illustration quality gate: illustration becomes a human layer integrated into a designed world, not a stock vector pasted into a card.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Dogstudio Zoox case study

Zoox is useful because the actual product form generated the site’s frames, borders and buttons. The transferable lesson is not rounded corners; it is that product truth can generate the shape grammar. Personality does not have a physical object, so its native geometry should come from evidence/artifacts, continuous dimensions, profiles and relationships. If a shape exists solely because it is fashionable, it should not survive the anti-copy test. This case also documents collaborative design/technical decision-making, reinforcing that feasibility should be discussed while the concept is being resolved rather than after static design is “finished.”

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Dogstudio Virgin Galactic case study

This case is relevant to our scene architecture because it had to bridge heavy immersive sequences and content-dense sections while minimizing performance/loading impact. Dogstudio discusses multiple WebGL subscenes, a shared render architecture and transitions as a technical priority. Personality does not need WebGL, but it absolutely needs the same boundary discipline: when Work Worlds releases into Context, or Context carries into Living Profile, the transition must be planned as a first-class system. The case also shows that optimizing perception often means precomputing/using cheaper visual techniques rather than insisting on realtime fidelity—an important progressive-enhancement lesson.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Dogstudio Geske case study

Geske uses visualization to explain product features that would otherwise be difficult to understand. The transferable rule is that special effects earn their cost by explaining. Personality charts and scroll transitions should similarly make a relationship clearer: which evidence becomes a question, how one signal enters four readings, how career fit decomposes. If an animation only makes a panel feel “alive,” it does not meet this bar.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — White Desert live/reference research

White Desert remains the strongest image-world reference because it lets the environment persist while foreground content changes. The visual lesson is not “use full-screen photos.” It is how image scale, sequence and environmental continuity reduce the feeling of disconnected sections. Personality can create a conceptual rather than literal persistent world: professional evidence keeps reappearing in different forms. White Desert also demonstrates that a restrained UI can let photography carry color, supporting the Phase 3C option to remove a dominant marketing accent.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — GSAP ScrollTrigger official documentation

ScrollTrigger remains the strongest tool for precise pinned scenic timelines because pinning, scrubbed progress, labels/snap behavior, refresh mechanics and directional progress are first-class. The most important engineering note for our implementation is to pin a stable stage and animate its descendants rather than transforming the pinned root. We also need to avoid high-frequency layout reads in `onUpdate` where possible; timeline measurements should be prepared and refreshed. GSAP is the owner of flagship theatre, not a universal hover/reveal library.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Lenis official documentation

Lenis provides smooth interpolation while retaining native-scroll semantics and offers direct GSAP integration. For Personality it is a candidate replacement for ScrollSmoother on fine-pointer marketing routes, not a required brand feature. We must validate anchor links, focus, route restoration, pin behavior, nested scroll areas and low-powered devices. Touch stays native by default. If Lenis fails those tests, native scroll is preferable to a “smoother” experience with accessibility or control problems.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Anime.js v4 documentation

Anime.js has a real timeline and scroll observer/synchronization system and therefore deserves to be understood rather than ignored. However, knowing a tool is not the same as assigning it a job. Phase 3C does not currently identify a flagship sequence where Anime is clearly superior to existing GSAP. Its likely role is isolated SVG/typographic/illustration choreography where its API makes the local animation cleaner. It must never own the same transform/progress state as GSAP in one scene.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Motion for React documentation

Motion is much better aligned with authenticated UI state than with our pinned marketing theatre. Its layout/layoutId features can animate real React layout changes, remain interruptible and support shared-element continuity. That makes it ideal for career list→detail, active tabs/lenses, dashboard rearrangements, dialogs, accordions and selection states. This helps separate “product motion” from “narrative scroll motion,” reducing the temptation to run everything through ScrollTrigger.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Native View Transition API / React Router integration

View Transitions can create route/shared-element continuity with graceful fallback in unsupported environments. They are useful when old/new DOM differs significantly and a snapshot transition is acceptable, for example carrying a selected career thumbnail/title into detail. They are not a replacement for interruptible in-page layout animation or scroll theatre. Our architecture should prefer React Router’s own integration instead of layering Barba over the SPA.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — CSS scroll-driven animations

Native scroll/view timelines are strategically useful for noncritical progressive effects, especially simple progress/opacity/translate relationships. Their existence does not justify moving core storytelling away from a well-tested JS owner before browser support and debugging behavior are sufficient. We can use them where failure means “the subtle effect does not run,” not where failure means “the visitor cannot understand the product sequence.”

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Embla Carousel

Embla proves that a small carousel engine can support variable widths, horizontal/vertical axes, drag-free behavior, progress and accessibility plugins without requiring a giant UI framework. Our default remains native scroll snap because it is lighter and directly manipulable. Embla becomes justified only if Work Worlds mobile or another gallery requires coordinated selected state, progress, velocity or snapping that native CSS cannot deliver reliably enough.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Rive runtime/state-machine research

Rive can package vector art and state machines into an interactive illustration system and has official React runtimes. The tradeoff is meaningful runtime/WASM weight, especially for richer renderers. Personality should therefore use static SVG/DOM by default. Rive is interesting if we later commission an authored “evidence becomes profile” illustration whose states genuinely respond to product data or user interaction. It is not justified for looping character motion.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Apple HIG — charts/layout/color

Apple’s guidance reinforces three Phase 3C principles: information importance should influence space; chart type follows the comparison/trend/relationship question; and color should be used consistently and not carry multiple meanings. It also supports progressive disclosure on smaller screens. The relevant transfer is not Apple visual styling but task clarity: one mobile state should own attention, and a chart should communicate without forcing hover or deciphering a legend.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Carbon data visualization/dashboard guidance

Carbon is useful as a disciplined operational reference. It distinguishes lollipop/radar/line/bar/part-to-whole chart purposes and emphasizes dashboard hierarchy. This validates our representation map and the move away from six equal MetricCards. Carbon is not the target skin; its value is semantic chart selection, labeling, accessibility and dense information behavior.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Responsive image / picture guidance

The web platform explicitly supports art direction through `<picture>` and different sources/crops. This makes the “desktop image squeezed into phone” failure avoidable. Our existing ResponsiveImage component already moves in this direction; Phase 3C must enrich the media registry so mobile crop is a deliberate creative decision rather than a filename convention. Intrinsic dimensions and responsive sizes also protect layout stability.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Mona Sans variable typography research

Mona Sans provides weight, width and optical axes broad enough to create distinct marketing, reading, UI and data roles inside one family. The lesson is not that Mona Sans is universally premium; it is that we can author character through axis behavior rather than introducing multiple fashionable faces. The font still requires real-string testing, especially long adaptive questions and dense chart labels.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

## C47 — Pexels / illustration licensing research

The production media system must include rights/provenance rather than treating a URL as enough. Pexels broadly permits free commercial use and editing under its license, but recognizable people/property/trademarks can still raise context-specific issues and cannot imply endorsement. Illustration sources differ: DrawKit and ManyPixels are attractive for no-attribution commercial use, while Storyset’s free route typically requires attribution. Exact licensing is checked again at selection time because terms can change.

**Personality-specific decision:** This source is used only for the principle described above. Before any future implementation step, re-check the current version of the source if the technology, compatibility, license or live behavior could have changed.

# C48. Mobile composition micro-specifications — 390px primary canvas

### C48 — Opening viewport

Header height is compact; brand + Menu/Sign in action remains clear. Headline uses 2–4 authored lines depending final copy. Architecture crop occupies a meaningful visual block without pushing all CTA content beyond the first useful scroll. The human/laptop fragment is smaller and either overlaps the lower edge deliberately or appears as the next beat. No horizontal media carousel appears simply because desktop has multiple actors.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Work Worlds active state

One work photograph dominates, around 78–86vw depending crop, with a visible next peek. World name and one sentence are immediately adjacent. Swipe/drag has direct previous/next or accessible index. Momentum does not leave the user between two unreadable panels. The phone does not simulate a 4000px pinned desktop sequence.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Context intake

A compact document/evidence preview uses the full content width minus 18–20px gutters. Evidence groups are rows/tags only when semantically correct, with edit affordances. The user can proceed or change source without navigating a horizontal composition.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Assessment question

Question number/progress sits in a quiet header; question text begins near the top third rather than dead center if it is long. Answer choices are reachable without precision. Continue/Back respect the software keyboard and safe area. Save/Exit is available but visually subordinate.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Living Profile

A lens selector can be horizontally compact or a dropdown/segmented control depending width. One visualization owns the stage. Labels below the radar/lollipop are readable without pinch zoom. Swiping between lenses is optional; buttons always work.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Dashboard zero state

User/context name, active-session status or zero count, illustration and primary action appear before secondary explanation. The illustration is cropped/positioned as an editorial actor, not a huge image pushing the action below multiple screens.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Dashboard populated

Latest profile/next action first; one Big Five plot; one career direction summary. Secondary readings and history follow. The bottom nav stays visible but does not cover chart labels or buttons.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Analytics

Each visualization is a dedicated module with readable axes/labels. Multi-series trend may use a dimension selector or small multiples rather than forcing five colored lines into a 320px plot. A “View data” action exposes the accessible table.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Career Explorer

Role list is the first view. Each row provides role name, truthful fit/status and maybe one concise reason. Selecting opens detail; shared-element transition is optional. Back returns to the exact list position.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Privacy

Controls are grouped by consequence, with full-width buttons only where action deserves it. Destructive account deletion is separated and confirmed. Explanatory text is compact enough that controls remain discoverable.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

### C48 — Footer

Terminal phrase uses mobile-specific line breaks and perhaps reduced size. Links are grouped with 44px targets. Any giant desktop type treatment that would cause overflow is explicitly replaced rather than clipped.

**Mobile rejection test:** Does this feel like an intentional app/page at 390px, or can the desktop layout be inferred as a stack underneath it? If the latter is true, redesign again.

# C49. Dashboard information architecture — zero, one-result, and longitudinal maturity states

The dashboard should evolve with data maturity instead of rendering one fixed grid and replacing dashes with numbers. Three maturity modes are explicitly designed.

## C49.1 — Maturity 0: no completed assessments

The dashboard explains what the user can do now, not what the future data might look like. Show real account/CV/session state, a quality illustration, the first-assessment action and the four reading families as an educational preview. Do not show empty trait charts as if 0 were a score. If an active assessment exists, Resume becomes the primary action and the current progress can be shown truthfully.

## C49.2 — Maturity 1: one completed assessment

The page can now show a real current profile but cannot honestly imply trends. Big Five, RIASEC, Work Values, Career Signals, top career relationships and next roadmap action are useful. “Change over time” is replaced by an invitation/explanation that longitudinal comparison unlocks after a future valid assessment. The page should feel complete rather than like half of the dashboard is disabled.

## C49.3 — Maturity 2+: longitudinal history

Trend visualization becomes legitimate. The dashboard may show a compact change summary and the Analytics route becomes richer. Even here, avoid interpreting small score changes as psychological “growth” unless methodology supports that claim. Lines show recorded values and dates; explanatory text remains cautious. History, report generation and roadmap completion become meaningful secondary evidence.

## C49.4 — Maturity-sensitive composition rule

The number and type of visible dashboard regions can change by maturity state. This is preferable to preserving a template grid with empty placeholders. Layout continuity comes from typography, navigation, data visualization language and identity—not from forcing the same rectangles to remain forever.

# C50. Phase 3C “done enough to code” threshold

Phase 3C research is sufficiently mature for implementation **only after** the remaining visual-resource approvals are resolved. General research has reached diminishing returns; the remaining uncertainty is concrete: final palette, exact third hero fragment if any, final Work Worlds/career imagery, exact illustration family/scenes, and any copy/data source blockers such as retention/question count. Once those are approved, the implementation prompt should be generated immediately rather than returning to generic inspiration browsing.

The next Codex run should be a rebuild, not a sequence of patches. It should preserve product/backend logic and source integrity while replacing rejected macro composition. The brief should be long enough to remove ambiguity. The implementation agent should be forbidden to make convenience substitutions such as “if image unavailable use placeholder,” “if chart data missing render zero,” “if mobile difficult stack desktop cards,” or “if motion difficult fade between states.” A blocker must be reported, not cosmetically hidden.


# C51. Final implementation-authority checklist and ecosystem transfer notes

This final appendix exists to prevent the Phase 3C decisions from becoming another research document that implementation only partially follows. It defines the questions the design authority must answer immediately before issuing any new Codex instruction set, and the questions the reviewer must answer immediately after the rendered implementation returns.

## C51.1 — Before writing the Codex prompt

Confirm that the **current Master Brain has actually been reopened**, not merely remembered. Search the document for the route, interaction, image type, dashboard representation and mobile state involved. Re-read the newest rejection ledger entries. A previous decision can become stale when the user rejects its visible consequence; therefore “the Brain once said this was okay” is not sufficient if Phase 3C superseded it.

Confirm the **current repository state**. The prompt must name the branch/commit or at least state that Codex is to inspect current `main` before editing. If another agent has changed media files, route structure, data hooks, CSS ownership, backend contracts or dependencies since the last audit, the design brief must adapt to the source truth rather than assume the old file map is unchanged.

Confirm that every visible **number and claim has provenance**. A dashboard field can display `0` when zero is the actual value; it cannot display zero because a JavaScript fallback converted missing data to zero. Public illustrative values are clearly labelled illustrative. Career fit, evidence confidence, report availability, scoring validity, roadmap completion and assessment counts are all rendered from their actual contracts. Marketing copy does not imply diagnosis, scientific validation, certainty, hiring fitness or privacy guarantees that have not been verified.

Confirm that every **visual resource is in the correct approval state**. `APPROVED` assets can be implemented. `SHORTLISTED` assets can be shown for review but must not silently ship as final. `REJECTED` assets are forbidden. `PENDING` means the design brief must either stop at that resource gate or explicitly use a temporary local research frame that will not be mistaken for final art direction. Coding agents do not get to convert “pending” into “I chose something similar.”

Confirm the **actor budget** for every scene. A scene should name its protagonist, supporting actors and quiet information. If the list is too long to describe clearly, the composition is probably too crowded. Hero actor count, Work Worlds active/neighbor count, dashboard primary/secondary zones and mobile state ownership are all explicit.

Confirm **inline and block geometry**. Width alone is never enough for a pinned or media-dominant scene. State the intended width range, maximum viewport height, aspect/crop logic, position, surrounding text area and what should remain visible in the same viewport. Test the mental frame at 1366×768 before coding.

Confirm **mobile as an independent composition**.
